## 0 · The year in one paragraph

2025 is the year this business went from nearly dormant to trading. Revenue went from **26,009.76**
to **271,840.58** — **ten times** *(the `Sales` line alone is thirteen)* — as it took on subcontract spray-foam insulation work for
general contractors in Connecticut. In September 2025 it onboarded Gusto, added employees and
**missed its first payroll**; no payroll appears to have run at all in the year, and no wages
account has ever existed in its books. Three vehicles/equipment items were capitalised for the
first time, with **no depreciation recorded**. The owners moved large sums both ways through a
**single pooled capital account** — 🛑 **SUPERSEDED THREE TIMES — the current answer (Lilian, relaying Julia's notes, 2026-09-03): TWO shareholders at 50% each, Ishkhan and Artem; TWO K-1s, Ishkhan's marked final (§4 decision 21).** ~~the final word (Julia, 2026-09-02 evening): ISHKHAN is the 100% shareholder for all of 2025 and Artem's first year is 2026. One shareholder, ONE K-1, to ISHKHAN (blocker B-0)~~ ⚠️ *(the client file's 2026-01-01 was right all along)* (a position that ended
1 January 2026, when the second owner exited over the nonresident-shareholder rule). Book net
income is **31,872.96**, against a 2024 loss of **(89,292.05)**.

### 2A · JE `2025-ADJ 2` — the Audi Jacksonville refund, moved to the account it belongs in

✅ **POSTED by Lilian, 2026-09-03.**

**What was wrong.** On **2025-06-05** the company received a **1,087.94** credit-card credit from
**Audi Jacksonville**, a European-marque dealership, and it was categorised to `Auto
Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel` (42). **It is a refund of repair work, not a fuel purchase**, and the
charge it refunds — **2,113.49** to the same vendor — sits in `Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair,
supplies and others` (43). Coded as it was, **fuel was understated by 1,087.94 and repairs overstated
by the same**, so the Audi Jacksonville relationship for the year read as 2,113.49 of cost when the
real figure is **1,025.55**.

**Fecha `12/31/2025` · Nº `JE 2025-ADJ 2` · ☑️ `Is Adjusting Journal Entry?`**

| # | ACCOUNT | DEBIT | CREDIT | **DESCRIPTION** *(type this on the line)* |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| **1** | `Auto Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel` | **1,087.94** | | `Reverses the Audi Jacksonville refund of 06/05/2025 miscoded here - it is a repair refund, not fuel` |
| **2** | `Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair, supplies and others` | | **1,087.94** | `Audi Jacksonville refund 06/05/2025 moved here, against the 2,113.49 repair charge it refunds` |

```
2025 correction - the Audi Jacksonville refund was coded to fuel, not to
repairs.

WHAT HAPPENED. On 06/05/2025 the company received a 1,087.94 credit-card
credit from Audi Jacksonville, a European-marque dealership. It was
categorised to Auto Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel. It is a refund of repair
work, not a fuel purchase, and the charge it refunds - 2,113.49 to the same
vendor - sits in Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair, supplies and others. Coded as
it was, fuel was understated by 1,087.94 and repairs overstated by the same,
so the Audi Jacksonville relationship for the year read as 2,113.49 of cost
when the real figure is 1,025.55.

WHY IT IS CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN BY RE-CATEGORISING THE ORIGINAL. So the
change is visible in the books themselves. Re-categorising gives the right
balance but leaves no trace anyone would find without opening the audit log.
The original transaction is left exactly as it was posted.

EFFECT. No cash moves. Auto Expenses in total is unchanged at 15,136.25 - only
the split between two of its sub-accounts moves. This entry does not change
the 2025 tax return, which reports Auto Expenses as a single figure. It is
posted alongside JE 2025-ADJ 3, which moves the remaining 1,692.40 of
European-marque cost to the shareholder.

Prepared by JK Accounting Group, 2026-09-03.
```

#### 🔑 WHY THIS IS AN ENTRY AND NOT A RE-CATEGORISATION — Lilian's rule, and it is a firm rule now

> 🔑 **Lilian, 2026-09-03:** *"quisiera mantenerlo en esa cuenta por motivos de trazabilidad… si una
> persona viene después y mira estos libros, especialmente Julia, entienda qué pasó, porque si
> simplemente la recategorizo, no va a quedar evidencia."*

⚠️ **One premise in that needed correcting, and the correction makes her instinct STRONGER, not
weaker: QuickBooks DOES record the edit.** Re-categorising writes to the **Audit History** — who,
when, which field, old value → new value. *(This paper has already used that screen: it is how JE
2025-ADJ was traced to Julia on 2026-04-27.)* 🔑 **So the problem is not that no evidence exists. It
is that the evidence sits where nobody will look.** Julia opening these books next year is not going
to think *"let me check the audit history of a June transaction"*.

ⓘ **THE NUMBERS IN THIS PAPER ARE LILIAN'S, TAKEN FROM THE POSTED BOOKS — `JE 2025-ADJ 2` for the
correction and `JE 2025-ADJ 3` for the reclassification.** This paper had proposed `2025-ADJ-2A` and
`2025-ADJ-2`; ✅ **hers is better** — two plain sequential numbers after Julia's `2025-ADJ`, with no
suffix to misread and no chance of the collision the earlier draft nearly created. **The books are the
authority on what an entry is called**, and every reference here was rewritten to match them.

🔑 **THE RULE THAT COMES OUT OF IT — and it is not specific to this client:**
**A miscategorisation is corrected by RE-CATEGORISING when the books are still working material, and
by a VISIBLE JOURNAL ENTRY once they have been reported on or a return is being built from them.**
The second leaves the wrong posting and its correction both on the face of the books, which is the
point. ⛔ **And whichever route is taken, it is ONE DECISION PER ENTRY** — which is why this
correction is `JE 2025-ADJ 2` and the tax reclassification is `JE 2025-ADJ 3`, rather than one combined
entry that cannot be reversed by halves.
📌 **Written up as a firm procedure at
[`projects/sops/correcting-entries.md`](../../sops/correcting-entries.md).**

ⓘ **The `Is Adjusting Journal Entry?` box is ticked on both.** It costs nothing, changes no figure,
and puts every adjustment the firm made into **one report** — *Adjusting Journal Entries*, and the
*Adjusted Trial Balance*. 🔑 **That is what gives Julia somewhere to look without knowing to look**,
which is the whole of what Lilian asked for.

⚖️ **IT CHANGES NO FIGURE ON THE RETURN.** The return reports the whole of `Auto Expenses` as **one**
line in the page-1 line-20 statement — **the parent is 15,136.25 either way.** ✅ **What it does buy
is the post-entry check below: with it, account 43 lands on the 4,059.65 this paper identifies row by
row; without it, on 5,147.59, which means nothing on its own.**

🛑 **SUPERSEDES "FIX 1"**, which this paper carried as a one-field recategorisation (§D-6 STEP 1).
**Same end state, different route, and the route was Lilian's decision.**

### 2B · JE 2025-ADJ 3 — the personal-vehicle reclassification

**What it does, in one sentence:** it takes the vehicle costs the company cannot have incurred —
there is no European car on its balance sheet — out of the expense accounts and charges them to the
shareholder as a distribution. **No cash moves.** The evidence behind every line is §D-8; the
in-and-out reconciliation behind line 4 is §D-10.

#### 🧾 The entry as it is TYPED — five lines, each with its own description

**QuickBooks Online → + New → Journal entry.** Header: **Journal date `12/31/2025`** · **Journal no.
`JE 2025-ADJ 3`** · ☑️ **`Is Adjusting Journal Entry?`**. ⛔ **Not `JE 2025-ADJ 2`** — that number belongs
to §2A, and two entries sharing one number is exactly the confusion the numbering exists to prevent.

| # | ACCOUNT | DEBITS | CREDITS | **DESCRIPTION** *(type this on the line)* | NAME |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| **1** | `Owner's distribution` | **3,341.08** | | `Sum of the 4 credits below - personal vehicle costs paid for Ishkhan Markarian, sole 2025 shareholder` | **Ishkhan Markarian** |
| **2** | `Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair, supplies and others` | | **1,692.40** | `Euro-marque parts left: 10,236.75 yr - 7,456.41 (JE 2025-ADJ) - 1,087.94 refund (JE 2025-ADJ 2)` | — |
| **3** | `Software` | | **175.00** | `Partslink24 Euro OEM parts catalogue: 5 monthly charges x 35.00, Apr-Aug 2025` | — |
| **4** | `Taxi or shared rides` | | **1,447.68** | `Turo: 14 charges 3,418.80 less 5 refunds and reversals 1,971.12 = 1,447.68 net, Feb-Aug 2025` | — |
| **5** | `Auto insurance` | | **26.00** | `MBA Rental Insurance 03/12/2025, bought the same day as two Turo rentals` | — |
| | **TOTAL** | **3,341.08** | **3,341.08** | | |

🔑 **Why each description is written that way — it is one rule, applied five times: the line must let
anyone re-derive the amount without this file.** Line 2 carries its whole subtraction **and names the
two earlier entries by their number**, so a reader in account 43 can walk the chain `JE 2025-ADJ` →
`JE 2025-ADJ 2` → this one without asking anybody. Line 3 carries the multiplication *(5 × 35.00)*.
Line 4 carries the **subtraction and its result** — which is the single thing that stops the next
reader "correcting" 1,447.68 up to 3,418.80. Line 5 carries the date that makes 26.00 mean something.
**Line 1 names whose distribution it is**, because the account is pooled across years and shareholders
and the name is the only thing that separates them.

🔴 **AND LINE 4's WORDING WAS CHANGED 2026-09-03 BECAUSE LILIAN DID NOT UNDERSTAND IT — which is the
test, not a complaint.** It read *"Turo net of reversals"*; she asked what a reversal was. ⛔ **If the
person pasting the description cannot read it, nobody opening these books in two years will either**
— and these descriptions exist for exactly that reader. The replacement **shows the subtraction and
its result** instead of labelling it, says **"refunds and reversals"** rather than the term alone, and
drops the bare word *"credits"*, which inside a journal entry collides with the credit column.
🔑 **A description a reader has to ask about has failed, however accurate it is.**
⚠️ **Keep them to roughly one line of text.** They have to stay readable in the **Journal report** and
in the account register, where a long description is cut off — and a description that is cut off in the
middle of a subtraction is worse than a short one.

ⓘ **The `NAME` column on line 1 is optional and depends on the file:** set it to **Ishkhan Markarian**
if he exists in QuickBooks as a name. **If he does not, leave it blank** — do **not** create a new
vendor for this. The description already names him, and that is what the return relies on.

#### 📝 The MEMO — the header field at the bottom of the entry

```
2025 personal-vehicle reclassification - distribution to ISHKHAN MARKARIAN,
sole shareholder for the whole of 2025 (the transfer to Artem Markarian is
effective at the close of 12/31/2025).

WHY. The company paid parts, service, an OEM parts-catalogue subscription,
Turo rentals and rental insurance for European passenger cars. Its entire
fleet is a 2014 Isuzu NPR box truck, a 2017 RAM ProMaster van and a 2006
Nissan forklift - 26,700.00 of fixed assets at 12/31/2025 - and no European
vehicle appears there, on the 2023 or 2024 depreciation schedules, or under
any 2025 lease or long-term rental. Julia ruled on 09/01/2026 that these cars
and the whole Turo relationship are personal to the family. Personal costs an
S corporation pays for its shareholder are distributions, not deductible
expenses (IRC 262 and 1368).

ORDER. This entry is posted after JE 2025-ADJ 2 of the same date, which
returned the 1,087.94 Audi Jacksonville refund from fuel to the repairs
account where the charge it refunds sits. The 1,692.40 credited below is net
of that refund. Read the three entries together: JE 2025-ADJ (Julia,
04/27/2026), JE 2025-ADJ 2 and this one.

NOT INCLUDED, deliberately. The 8,847.00 of vehicle finance, 7,456.41 of
vehicle repairs and 1,804.12 already moved to this account by JE 2025-ADJ on
12/31/2025 - repeating them would double-count. And 1,192.24 of fuel, parking
and insurance premiums that look personal but rest on inference rather than
evidence; they stay as company expense until the insurance declaration pages
are seen.

EFFECT. No cash moves. Deductible expenses fall and ordinary income rises by
3,341.08; distributions rise by the same amount, so under IRC 1367 stock basis
is unchanged and no capital gain arises. Cost of goods sold and Form 1125-A
are untouched.

Prepared by JK Accounting Group, 2026-09-03, for the 2025 Form 1120-S
(cash basis, following the 2024 return). Full working paper on file.
```

ⓘ **This is the version to paste.** §D-8 carries a longer one, written before the entry was final; it
is **the file record and the source of the report to Julia**, not the field text. **If the memo field
will not take this much, cut from the bottom up** — `EFFECT` first, then `NOT INCLUDED` — and never the
first two paragraphs, which are the *what* and the *why*.

### 2C · The 1,000.00 `Zelle payment to Artem` — moved out of contributions, into distributions

✅ **CORRECTED by Lilian, 2026-09-03**, by recategorising the transaction itself *(not by a journal
entry — the books had not been reported on for this one, and it is a single row)*.

| | |
|---|---|
| **The transaction** | **2025-02-13** · type **Expense** · **1,000.00** · `Zelle payment to Artem` · paid from the Chase operating account |
| **Where it WAS** | 🔴 `Owner's contribution` (48), as a **NEGATIVE** — the only negative row among the 51 in that account |
| **Where it IS now** | ✅ `Owner's distribution` (49) |
| **Why** | **money leaving the company for the owner is a DISTRIBUTION, not an un-contribution.** Netting it inside the contribution account made both equity figures understate the year |

🔑 **It satisfies §4 decision 17's own test, so it is not an exception to it.** The rule is that
nothing Julia categorised moves unless it is **necessary** or an **obvious** error. **A single negative
row among fifty-one positives, in an account whose entire purpose is money coming IN, is the obvious
case.** ⚖️ **Recorded as §4 decision 19**, and it **resolves §D-9 note 2**, which had left it as posted.

**What it does to the figures:**

| | Before | **After** |
|---|---:|---:|
| `Owner's contribution` — FY2025 movement | 100,077.40 | **101,077.40** |
| `Owner's distribution` — FY2025 movement, before JE 2025-ADJ 3 | 115,722.95 | **116,722.95** |
| `Owner's distribution` — **after** JE 2025-ADJ 3 | 119,064.03 | 🔴 **120,064.03** |

🛑 **ON THE RETURN, EXACTLY TWO FIGURES MOVE: Schedule K line 16d and K-1 box 16 code D**, from
119,064.03 to **120,064.03** (§3J, §3N). **Everything else is unchanged, because the two sides offset:**

| Figure | Effect | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Schedule L line 23, paid-in capital | **70,682 — unchanged** | ƒ `88,464 + 101,077.40 + 1,204.12 − 120,064.03 = 70,681.49` — the same as before, because contributions and distributions each rose by 1,000.00 |
| Total assets · total liabilities · total equity | **unchanged** | nothing left or entered the company; one equity account moved to another |
| Page 1 line 22, Schedule M-1, Schedule M-2 | **unchanged** | it is equity-to-equity. ⓘ **M-2 is untouched twice over**: this client's convention keeps contributions off line 3, and line 7 is zero because the AAA is negative |
| 🔑 **§3P step 3 — the room the distributions have to fit into** | **21,358.55 — unchanged** | ƒ `101,077.40 + 40,345.18 − 120,064.03`. 🔑 **So the depreciation threshold §3H measures against does not move either.** ⚠️ **This is the ENTITY aggregate. Since §4 decision 21 there are TWO shareholders and §3P computes it TWICE — 10,679.27 each on the 50/50 assumption** |
| Ending stock basis | **14,287.41 — unchanged** | same reason. ⚠️ **Entity aggregate; per shareholder it is 7,143.70 / 7,143.71 (§3P)** |

✅ **AND IT UNBLOCKS A TEST THAT COULD NOT BE RUN BEFORE.** §D-9 note 2 recorded that the equity
movements were reported **net**, and that the firm's netting policy
*([`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §5C-v)* **tests the GROSS split,
so a netted figure cannot be run through its own gate.** **The figures are now gross and the gate does
run — and it still fails:** distributions **116,722.95** exceed contributions **101,077.40**. ⚖️ **The
conclusion is unchanged — the return is prepared GROSS — but it is now measured on the right
numbers.**

### 2D · JE `2025-ADJ 4` — the 2025 depreciation, all three assets

🟢 **Given to Lilian 2026-09-04**, after the ATX asset register was read off the software (§3H).
🔑 **This is the entry that closes the last open figure on the return.**

#### ✅ THE ACCOUNTS — Lilian's structure, and it is better than the one this paper first proposed

🟢 **BUILT BY LILIAN 2026-09-04, and adopted.** She created, under **each** asset parent, the two
sub-accounts QuickBooks' own fixed-asset wizard uses:

```
2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage            Fixed Assets     <- the parent shows NET BOOK VALUE
  |- Original cost                  Fixed Assets     <- what it cost. Never moves again.
  |- Depreciation                   Fixed Assets     <- accumulated. Grows every year.
Depreciation Expense           Other Expense    <- SEPARATE, top-level, ONE for all three
```

🔑 **Why this beats the flat `<asset> — Accumulated Depreciation` accounts this section first
proposed:** the parent shows net book value on one line, cost and wear sit together under the asset,
a disposal reads off two lines, **and it is the convention the chart already used** for the 2022 RAM.

🔑 **AND ONE METHOD LESSON FROM THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS SECTION, kept because the header refers to it.**
That draft said the books had **no** depreciation accounts and told Lilian to create two. ⛔ **Both
halves were false**, and the cause was the check: it ran against the **2025 General Ledger's POSTING
accounts**, which by construction list only accounts that **carried a transaction that year** —
`Depreciation Expense` had a balance in 2024 and none in 2025, so it was invisible. 🛑 **A
chart-of-accounts question is answered from the CHART** *(`get_transaction_accounts`, including
inactive)*, **never from a general ledger.**

🛑 **AND THE ONE THING THAT HAS TO BE SAID OUT LOUD, because it cost two wrong postings:**
⛔ **`Depreciation Expense` CANNOT be a sub-account of the asset.** QuickBooks requires a sub-account
to share its parent's **type**, and the parent is `Fixed Assets`. **That is not a limitation — it is
correct**: an expense lives in the **P&L** and the asset lives in the **balance sheet**. They are two
different statements.

| | `Depreciation Expense` | `<asset>:Depreciation` |
|---|---|---|
| Account type | 🔴 **Other Expense** *(detail type: Depreciation)* — **NOT "Expenses"**; see the note below | **Fixed Assets** |
| Statement | **Profit and Loss** | **Balance Sheet** |
| Position | 🔴 **top-level, NOT under any asset** | a sub-account of the asset |
| How many | 🔴 **ONE, shared by all three** | **one per asset** — three |
| Measures | **this year's** wear | the **accumulated** wear since purchase |
| The entry | takes the **DEBIT** | takes the **CREDIT** |

🛑 **THE ACCOUNT TYPE IS `Other Expense`, AND THIS PAPER SAID `Expenses` UNTIL A REVIEW CAUGHT IT
2026-09-04.** ⛔ **The two are mutually exclusive in QuickBooks:** an `Expenses` account sits **inside
Total Expenses, above Net Operating Income**; an `Other Expense` account sits **below it**. 📖 **The
re-exported P&L puts `Depreciation Expense` below Net Operating Income**, under a *Other Expenses*
heading, with Total Expenses still at **156,084.23** — so the type is **Other Expense**. 📄 **2024 did
the same**: total expenses 75,202.81 → net operating income (58,374.27) → *then* the disposal loss and
`Depreciation Expense` 12,071.00 → net income (89,292.05). ✅ **Continuity with the prior year holds,
and it is the type — not the name — that decides where it lands.**

⚠️ **`Depreciation Expense` ALREADY EXISTED** and was reactivated, not recreated — it carried
**12,071.00 in 2024** and nothing in 2025. ⚖️ **The P&L position is CONSISTENT with it being the same
account, not proof of it** *(any new account of the same type renders in the same place — corrected
after review)*. **What actually establishes identity is the reactivation.**

#### 🛑 TWO WRONG VERSIONS WERE POSTED FIRST. Both are recorded, because the failure is instructive

| Version | What was posted | What it did |
|---|---|---|
| **1st** | DR `<asset>:Depreciation` · CR `<asset>:Original cost` | ⛔ **moved the cost sideways inside the balance sheet.** Nothing reached the P&L: net income stayed at 33,274.04, total fixed assets stayed at 26,700.00, `Original cost` emptied to zero and accumulated depreciation went **positive** |
| **2nd** | DR `Depreciation Expense` ✅ · CR `<asset>:Original cost` ⛔ | the debit was right; the credit still **emptied the cost account**. Totals would have looked right *(fixed assets 0, expense 26,700)* while **both sub-accounts read zero — the three assets would have vanished from the books with no record of what they cost** |
| **3rd** ✅ | DR `Depreciation Expense` · CR `<asset>:Depreciation` | ✅ **posted and verified** |

🔑 **The root cause of both, and the two rules that prevent them:**
> ⛔ **On THIS client, the DEBIT of a depreciation entry is never an asset account.** It crosses from
> the P&L to the balance sheet.
> ⛔ **`Original cost` NEVER appears in a DEPRECIATION entry.** *(It does move for a capital
> improvement, a price correction, a trade-in or an impairment — but never for depreciation.)*

⚠️ **The first rule is NOT universal, and the review was right to say so.** Where depreciation is on
assets used in **production**, it is capitalised into inventory or work-in-progress under §263A /
absorption costing, and depreciation on an asset used to **build another asset** is capitalised into
that asset — **the debit is then an asset account, correctly.** ✅ **It does not arise here**: this
client holds no inventory and runs a service trade. 🛑 **Do not carry the absolute version to a
manufacturer or a contractor with WIP.**

🛠️ **And the mechanical trap that produced it: the dropdown.** Typing "depreciation" offers **FIVE**
accounts, not four — one expense, three asset sub-accounts, **and a decoy**: `2022 RAM 2500 —
Accumulated Depreciation`, the empty shell of the vehicle disposed of in 2024 (§3H item ②), **which
looks exactly like the account you want and belongs to a truck the company no longer owns.**
⚠️ *(Whether that account is still ACTIVE has not been checked — if it is inactive it will not appear.
Check it; if it is active, consider making it inactive.)*
🔑 **The sub-accounts show the parent's name first** (`2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage:Depreciation`); the
expense account stands alone. **Read the path, not the word.**

#### The entry

**Date `12/31/2025` · Journal no. `2025-ADJ 4` · tick `Is Adjusting Journal Entry?`**

⚠️ **THIS TABLE IS THE ENTRY AS DRAFTED — six lines. AS POSTED it is FOUR**, because the three debit
lines were consolidated into one of 26,700.00 to `Depreciation Expense`. **The credits are as shown.**

| # | Account | **DÉBITO** | **CRÉDITO** | Description — the note on that line |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | `Depreciation Expense` | **13,000.00** | | `2014 Isuzu NPR box truck - placed in service 06/02/2025 - 100% bonus depreciation, entire cost deducted in 2025` |
| 2 | `Depreciation Expense` | **10,000.00** | | `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500 - placed in service 04/14/2025 - 100% bonus depreciation, entire cost deducted in 2025` |
| 3 | `Depreciation Expense` | **3,700.00** | | `2006 Nissan forklift - placed in service 08/25/2025 - 100% bonus depreciation, entire cost deducted in 2025` |
| 4 | `2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage:Depreciation` | | **13,000.00** | `2014 Isuzu NPR box truck - accumulated depreciation at 12/31/2025 - first year, no prior depreciation` |
| 5 | `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500:Depreciation` | | **10,000.00** | `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500 - accumulated depreciation at 12/31/2025 - first year, no prior depreciation` |
| 6 | `2006 Nissan Forklift:Depreciation` | | **3,700.00** | `2006 Nissan forklift - accumulated depreciation at 12/31/2025 - first year, no prior depreciation` |
| | **TOTAL** | **26,700.00** | **26,700.00** | |

ⓘ **As POSTED, the three debit lines were consolidated into ONE line of 26,700.00 to
`Depreciation Expense`** — which is fine and is what the books now carry: it is a single P&L account,
and the three credit lines already give the per-asset detail. ⚠️ **The per-line descriptions were left
EMPTY in the posted entry** *(the General Ledger export shows a blank description column on all four
lines)*. 📌 **§2's own rule — a note beside every debit and every credit — is not met until they are
filled in.** **Adding them is a one-field edit per line and changes no figure.**

#### The header memo

```
2025 depreciation - the three assets placed in service during 2025.

WHAT THIS IS.   First-year depreciation on the only three depreciable assets the
                company owns. None of them had ever been depreciated: the 2024
                fixed-asset register is empty and every prior-depreciation field
                in the tax software reads zero before this entry.

THE ASSETS.     2014 Isuzu NPR box truck, in service 06/02/2025, cost 13,000.00.
                2017 RAM ProMaster 3500, in service 04/14/2025, cost 10,000.00
                (3,000.00 paid in cash on 04/14/2025, 7,000.00 financed and booked
                against the 2017 RAM Loan).
                2006 Nissan forklift, in service 08/25/2025, cost 3,700.00.
                Total 26,700.00, which agrees line for line with the asset register
                in the tax software.

THE METHOD.     100% bonus depreciation (the special depreciation allowance) on all
                three, so the entire cost is deducted in 2025. No Section 179 is
                elected. The books are kept on the same basis as the tax return, so
                Schedule M-1 carries no depreciation difference.

ACCOUNTS.       Debit to the existing Depreciation Expense account, which last carried a
                balance in 2024 and was reactivated for this entry. Credits to a new
                Depreciation sub-account under each asset, beside its Original cost
                sub-account, which is the convention the chart already used.

EFFECT.         Depreciation expense 26,700.00 for 2025. Net book income falls from
                33,274.04 to 6,574.04. On the balance sheet the three assets stay at
                26,700.00 of cost with 26,700.00 of accumulated depreciation, so their
                net book value at 12/31/2025 is zero. On Form 1120-S, line 14 becomes
                26,700.00 and ordinary business income on line 22 becomes 13,645.18.

Prepared by JK Accounting Group, 2026-09-04.
```

#### ⚖️ Why book depreciation is set EQUAL to tax depreciation

**Bonus depreciation is a tax rule, not a book one** — under GAAP these assets would be written off
over five and seven years. **Booking the full 26,700.00 makes the books agree with the return**, which
is what keeps **Schedule M-1 line 3a at zero** (§3L) and what **2024 did** *(no book/tax depreciation
difference)*. 🔑 **These are tax-basis books for a small company and continuity with the prior year
governs.** ⛔ **The alternative — book straight-line, tax bonus — is not wrong, but it opens a
permanent M-1 difference that someone has to maintain every year for seven years, and nobody here has
been maintaining a fixed-asset schedule at all.**

| Asset | What the ledger actually holds | ATX register | ✅ |
|---|---|---|---|
| `2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage` | one payment, **06/02/2025**, `LNB 2670 LLC`, 13,000.00 | in service **06/02/2025**, cost 13,000 | ✅ date **and** cost match |
| `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500` | **04/14/2025** `Drago Solutions Inc.` 300.00 + 2,700.00, then **12/31/2025** journal entry 7,000.00 against `2017 RAM Loan` | in service **04/14/2025**, cost 10,000 | ✅ 3,000 cash + 7,000 financed = 10,000. **§3H's "confirm the ProMaster's 10,000 is the whole price" is CLOSED** |
| `2006 Nissan Forklift` ⚠️ **RENAMED** *(was `USED 2006 NISSAN FORKLIFT 4,000LB`)* | **07/30/2025** 500.00 + **08/26/2025** 3,200.00, `Mas Forklift & Parts Inc` | in service **08/25/2025**, cost 3,700 | ⚠️ cost matches; **the date matches neither payment** — see §3H item ③ |

### 2E · JE `2025-ADJ 5` — 18,986.63 of distributions reclassified to officer's compensation

🛑 **SUPERSEDED — THIS SECTION IS NOT THE RETURN. §3H-4 IS.** Every figure below is at an earlier state of this file *(one of: depreciation = 0 · §3H-2 at D = 26,700 · §3H-3 after `JE 2025-ADJ 5`)*. 🔴 **§3H-4 carries the column that goes on the form** — the compensation is all Artem's (§4 decision 24) and **Schedule K 16d and both K-1 box 16D are ZERO** (§4 decision 25). ⛔ **Do not key a figure from this section without checking it there first.**


⚠️ **POSTED BY LILIAN 2026-09-04; VERIFIED against the re-exported books 2026-09-05.** She asked how
to make the entry and how to create the account, and posted it.
🛑 **WHO DECIDED IT IS NOT ESTABLISHED IN THIS PAPER.** The SOP is explicit: *"A preparer does not
invent a salary, and does not reclassify distributions unilaterally… The decision belongs to whoever
signs the return, and it gets recorded"* ([`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md)
§5C-viii). **Whether this followed Julia's instruction is one sentence from her or Lilian, and it goes
in §4 decision 23.** Until then the entry stands in the books and the return is built on it.

#### The entry as POSTED — read off the General Ledger

| | Account | DÉBITO | CRÉDITO | Description on the line, as posted |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | `Officer's compensation` *(type Expenses — a NEW account; it renders under `Office Expense` on the P&L)* | **18,986.63** | | `2025 officer compensation - reclassified from Owner's distribution` |
| 2 | `Owner's distribution` *(Equity — the pooled account)* | | **18,986.63** | `Reclassified to Officer's Compensation` |

Date **12/31/2025** · number **`JE 2025-ADJ 5`** · ✅ **both lines carry a description**, per §2's rule.
⚠️ Whether `Is Adjusting Journal Entry?` was ticked is not visible in a General Ledger export — the
same loose end as §2D item ②.

#### Where the amount comes from — and what it is NOT

```
2025 distributions, gross (after §2C) ........ 120,064.03
2025 contributions, gross .................... 101,077.40
                                             -----------
excess of distributions over contributions ..  18,986.63   <- the entry, to the cent
```

**After the entry the two are EQUAL — 101,077.40 each way.**

🔴 **That is an EQUITY figure, not a compensation figure.** Reasonable compensation is what the
services were worth, and the firm's [`reasonable-compensation` skill](../../../.claude/skills/reasonable-compensation/)
exists to produce that number with its write-up. On the record as it stands, an examiner who asks
*"how was the salary determined?"* is answered *"it is the amount by which distributions exceeded
contributions."* 📌 **Recorded as a fact for the file, not argued** — §4 decision 23 puts it to Julia.

#### What it does to the RETURN — the summary; §3H-3 is the full column

1. **Page 1 line 7 = 18,986.63**, line 21 **194,699.72**, 🔴 **line 22 = (5,341.45) — the company now
   reports a LOSS.** *(Form 1125-E is not required — receipts are under 500,000.)*
2. **Schedule K 16d = 101,077.40**, K-1 box 16D **50,538.70 each**. Still **GROSS**: §5C-v gate 1 reads
   *"exceed"* and the two are equal; **gate 4 fails regardless** (2024 did not run contributions through
   M-2 line 3 — §3M).
3. 🔴 **K-1 box 1 = (2,670.73) / (2,670.72), and NEITHER can deduct it in 2025.** Each shareholder's
   basis after distributions is **exactly zero** *(50,538.70 in, 50,538.70 out, no income)*, so the loss
   is **suspended under §1366(d)** — 🛑 **Ishkhan's DIES**: his K-1 is final and a suspended loss does
   not survive the disposition of the stock; **Artem's carries to 2026** (Form 7203 Part III).
4. ✅ **The capital gain on the distributions is GONE** — 2,670.73 / 2,670.72 → **0 / 0**. Distributions
   no longer exceed basis; they equal it.
5. 🔴 **9,493.32 / 9,493.31 of COMPENSATION reaches each 1040 by a route NOTHING on this 1120-S carries**
   — no K-1 box holds salary. **It is the same 9,493.32 per head that used to arrive as 6,822.59 of
   ordinary income plus 2,670.73 of capital gain**, now all at ordinary rates **plus employment tax**
   *(≈1,341 each on the Schedule C route; ≈1,452 in FICA both halves on the W-2 route)* **and minus
   Artem's ≈1,365 of QBI deduction** *(his QBI is now a loss that carries forward; Ishkhan's deduction
   was already nil under his 2024 carryforward)*.

#### 🛑 The two things it does NOT settle — both to Julia, both recorded in §3S

| | The question | What turns on it |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **WHOSE compensation is it?** The credit went to the **pooled** `Owner's distribution` account — the same one-account-for-two-people problem as §3P. **The tables split it 50/50 like everything else** | 🔴 **If all 18,986.63 is ONE person's**, his distributions fall to **41,045.39** *(no gain; 9,493.31 of room; his loss and nondeductibles ARE absorbed)* — **and the OTHER's stay at 60,032.01 against 50,538.70 of basis: a capital gain of 9,493.31**, larger than either had before the entry |
| **2** | **The REPORTING ROUTE.** An officer is a statutory employee *(§3121(d)(1))*, so line 7 normally pairs with **W-2 · 941 · 940 · Connecticut withholding**, and no payroll ran | **Route (b), late payroll:** the company's half of FICA *(≈1,452)* is deductible → **line 12 rises, line 22 falls by the same, a payroll liability appears on Schedule L, and Statement A's W-2 wages become 9,493.32 / 9,493.31.** **The Masciave route** *(line 7 here; each owner reports his share on a Schedule C and pays SE tax)*: **nothing else on this return moves — the tables are built on it** because it is what the books as posted say |

🔗 **The SOP's own three routes are in §5C-viii** — (a) file as computed and document · (b) late-file
payroll and reclassify · (c) reclassify only owner payments already found. **What was posted is (b)'s
reclassification without (b)'s filings.** ⛔ **Not a preparer's call; recorded, not decided.**

# 🧮 THE ATX TABLES — Form 1120-S, FY2025, cash basis

### 3A · How to read every table below

| Column | What it holds |
|---|---|
| **Line** | the line **as printed on the form**, with its **page** in the section heading |
| **Concept** | the form's own wording, shortened |
| **Value** | 🔴 **the figure to key.** Cents are shown; ATX rounds to whole dollars on the printed return |
| **Where it came from** | 📖 = read off a report · ƒ = a formula · 📌 = a decision in §4 · 📄 = copied from the 2024 return |
| 🛠️ **Where it is ENTERED** | ✅ **typed on this form** · **computed — do not type** · or the *other* form it must be typed on |

⚠️ **The 🛠️ column is carried on the tables where the route is not obvious — §3D, §3F, §3G — and on
the two lines where the SOP says a first-time preparer gets stuck: Schedule M-1 line 3b and Schedule L
line 24, both flagged in place.** ⛔ **Everywhere else the rule is the plain one: the figure is typed on
the form it appears on**, except where a row says otherwise.

🛑 **A line that is not listed is ZERO and is left blank.** Both the income and the deduction tables
list every line the form has, including the zero ones, so no leftover account can find a home on a line
this paper never mentioned.

⚠️ **The question and line numbers are the 2025 form's** — the SOP's page map was verified against the
2025 `f1120s.pdf` on 2026-08-23. **This session could not re-pull the PDF** *(irs.gov is blocked from
this environment)*, so on **Schedule B** the questions below are given **by their wording**, which is
what the SOP tells you to match on anyway. **ATX prints the year in the top-right of every page — read
the number off the form face there.**

---

### 3B · The order of preparation — and the two circularities

**Prepare in this order. Each step needs the one before it.**

| # | Do this | Why it cannot come earlier |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | ✅ **DONE 2026-09-03 — `JE 2025-ADJ 2`, `JE 2025-ADJ 3` and the §2C recategorisation are POSTED** | every figure below is the books *after* those three changes, and §5 now ties them to the client's own re-exported P&L, Balance Sheet and General Ledger |
| **1** | **Form 1125-A** — cost of goods sold | page 1 line 2 is its line 8 |
| **2** | **Page 1, income** — lines 1a to 6 | needs 1125-A |
| **3** | **Form 4562** — depreciation ✅ **ANSWERED, POSTED AND VERIFIED 2026-09-04: 26,700.00 through BONUS** (§2D, §3H) | feeds page 1 line 14 **or** Schedule K line 11, depending on the election (§3H) |
| **4** | **Page 1, deductions** — lines 7 to 21, and the **line-20 statement** | needs 4562 |
| **5** | **Page 1 line 22** — ordinary business income | computed |
| **6** | **Schedule B** | independent, but its receipts question needs line 1a |
| **7** | **Schedule K** | line 1 is page 1 line 22 |
| **8** | **Schedule L** — assets and liabilities, lines 1 to 21 | independent of the income lines; needs the 2024 return for the opening column |
| **9** | **Schedule M-1** | needs book net income **and** the finished Schedule K |
| **10** | **Schedule M-2** | needs page 1 line 22, the nondeductibles and the distributions |
| **10b** | 🔑 **Schedule L, the EQUITY lines 22 to 27** | ⛔ **not at step 8.** Line 24 is a **book** figure that **the software normally computes from the M-2 WORKSHEET — including its bridge column** — rather than letting you type it *(SOP §10C)*. ⚠️ **The bridge column is the point: the AAA and retained earnings are not the same thing.** **The balance sheet does not close until the M-2 is done** |
| **11** | **Schedule K-1** + **Statement A** | allocation of the finished Schedule K |
| **12** | **Form 7203** — 🔗 **not part of this return.** It is Ishkhan's, on his 1040 (§3P) | needs the finished K-1 |

**⭕ The two circularities — where a figure depends on something that depends on it:**

1. 🔴 **Depreciation ↔ the §179 income limit.** A §179 deduction cannot exceed the business's taxable
   income computed *before* it. So you need line 22 to size §179, and §179 changes what line 22 means.
   ✅ **Here it does not bite:** income before any depreciation is **40,345.18**, comfortably above the
   26,700.00 of assets, so the whole cost is available under either route.
2. ⚠️ **Book depreciation ↔ Schedule L.** If the depreciation is **posted to the books**, Schedule L's
   accumulated-depreciation line moves and book net income falls. If it is **not** posted, Schedule L
   stays as built below and the difference appears on **Schedule M-1** instead. **The 2024 return posted
   it** *(books and return both show 12,071)* — 🔑 **so follow that: post it, and there is no M-1 item.**
   ⛔ **What is not allowed is mixing the two** *(the 1120-S SOP §5D says so explicitly)*.

---

### 3C · Where these forms live, and how to reach them in ATX

| Page | What is on it |
|---|---|
| **1** | header + items A–J · income 1a–6 · deductions 7–21 · **line 22 ordinary business income** · tax & payments 23–28 · signature |
| **2** | **Schedule B**, questions **1–11** |
| **3** | **Schedule B (continued) 12–17**, then **Schedule K begins**, lines **1–16f** |
| **4** | **Schedule K (continued) 17a–18**, then **the whole of Schedule L** |
| **5** | **Schedule M-1** and **Schedule M-2**, stacked |

🛠️ **In ATX:** the page buttons are at the bottom of the form window, next to **`Pages & Worksheets`**,
which lists every page *and* every supporting worksheet. **Form 1125-A, Form 4562 and each Schedule K-1
are separate forms** in the return's form list, not pages of the 1120-S. **Add them from `Add Forms`**
if ATX has not pulled them in automatically.

🛑 **And before you transmit: print the return and read the FORM LIST.** ATX attaches a form the moment
its parent line is touched, and **fixing the line does not detach the form.** A blank Form 4797 or
Schedule D left behind transmits with the return.

from somewhere, and the IRS wants the composition.

#### 🔄 The one presentation change from 2024, and it is deliberate

📄 **2024 used only three deduction lines — Rents (11), Depreciation (14) and Other deductions (20)** —
and put **everything else**, advertising included, into the line-20 statement.
🔑 **2025 breaks out lines 12, 13 and 16.** Reasons, in order of weight:
- **Advertising has its own line on the form**, and the SOP says in terms not to leave it in other
  deductions. In 2024 it was 845; **in 2025 it is 11,572.13 — 7% of total deductions.**
- Taxes-and-licences and interest likewise have named lines the IRS wants filled.
- ⚖️ **It changes NO figure.** Total deductions, ordinary income, the K-1 and every schedule are
  identical either way. **It is presentation.**

🛑 **It is still a change of convention, so it is recorded as a decision** *(§4 decision 18)*.
**If Lilian prefers exact continuity with 2024**, move all three back into the statement: line 20
becomes **149,013.09** and lines 12, 13 and 16 go to zero. **Nothing else moves.**

| Asset | Cost in the books |
|---|---:|
| `2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage` — Isuzu NPR box truck | **13,000.00** |
| `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500` — cargo van | **10,000.00** *(7,000.00 of it financed, through `JournalEntry-2025-ADJ` against the `2017 RAM Loan`)* |
| `2006 Nissan Forklift` ⚠️ **renamed 2026-09-04** *(was `USED 2006 NISSAN FORKLIFT 4,000LB`; §D-8 and §E-7b keep the old name because that is what it was called then)* | **3,700.00** |
| **Total** | **26,700.00** |

#### ✅ THE FOUR OPEN QUESTIONS, AND WHAT THE ATX REGISTER ANSWERS

**Read off the software 2026-09-04** *(three asset-entry screens, one per asset)*, then checked back
against the General Ledger transaction by transaction (§2D).

| # | What was missing | **The answer** |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 🔴 **DATE PLACED IN SERVICE** | **06/02/2025** *(Isuzu)* · **04/14/2025** *(ProMaster)* · **08/25/2025** *(forklift)*. ✅ **None falls in the fourth quarter, so the MID-QUARTER CONVENTION is not triggered** — Q2 carries 23,000.00 and Q3 carries 3,700.00, Q4 nothing. ATX applies **HY** to all three, which is right |
| 2 | 🔴 **THE ELECTION** | **`Section 179: 0`** and **`Qualified Special Allowance: 100%`** on all three → **the route is BONUS, not §179.** `Special Allowance Deduction` equals the full cost on each screen, so **D = 26,700.00** and it lands on **page 1 line 14**. ⚠️ **This paper still quotes no rate from memory** — the 100% is **what ATX itself computed** for these dates, read off the screen. ⛔ **irs.gov remains unreachable from this session, so the ACQUISITION-date cut-off behind that 100% has not been independently checked** — if any of the three was under a binding contract before the cut-off, the rate changes and so does every figure in §3H-2 |
| 3 | 🟡 **Recovery period** | **5.0 / 5.0 / 7.0** *(class 7 truck-van-auto twice, class 10 general-purpose tools once)*. ⓘ **It changes nothing this year** — 100% bonus writes off the whole cost whatever the class. 🔑 **It would matter the moment anyone elects OUT of bonus**, and that is where §3H's old warning still stands: for a construction trade Rev. Proc. 87-56 asset class 15.0 gives the forklift **5-year GDS**, not 7 |
| 4 | 🔴 **Business-use percentage — and the register does NOT close this one** | **`Bus percent: 100.00`** on all three, and **`COGS` unticked**. 🛑 **CORRECTED after review 2026-09-04.** ~~The qualified-nonpersonal-use analysis is therefore not reached this year~~ — ⛔ **that inverts the logic. Keying 100% is what TRIGGERS the substantiation question, not what answers it.** See the block immediately below |

#### 🔴 LISTED PROPERTY — the test the register does not run, and 2024 shows it is live here

🛑 **This is not theoretical for this client.** 📄 **The 2024 filed return put its only vehicle on Form
4562 PART V — LISTED PROPERTY**, at *"business/investment use 100.00%"* (§E). **So the prior preparer
either treated a company truck as listed property or let the software default it there — and either
way, Part V is where these three are likely to land unless someone decides otherwise.**

**Why it matters, and it is a signed assertion:** Part V asks *"Do you have evidence to support the
business/investment use claimed?"* and *"Is the evidence written?"* — **two Yes/No boxes that assert a
fact**, and ⛔ **no mileage log has ever been seen for this client.** And if any of the three were a
**passenger automobile**, §280F would **cap the first-year deduction** — which would make every figure
in §3H-2 wrong.

**The test to run, in this order:**

| Step | The question | On these facts |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Is it a **qualified nonpersonal-use vehicle**? §280F(d)(4)(C), Reg. §1.274-5T(k) — **then it is NOT listed property and needs no log at all** | ✅ **A forklift is not a "vehicle" under §280F in any case.** A **box truck** and a **cargo van** are the textbook examples of the exception — but the test is the vehicle's own facts: seating, permanent markings, **GVWR over 6,000 lb** |
| **2** | If the exception holds | **Part V is not used**, the evidence questions are not reached, and 100% business use needs no log |
| **3** | If it does NOT hold for one of them | that one goes in **Part V**, the evidence questions must be answered **truthfully**, and **§280F's caps apply if it is a passenger automobile** |

⛔ **Do not answer the Part V evidence questions "Yes" because 100.00% was keyed.** 🔑 **The percentage
is an input; the evidence question is about whether a record exists.** ✅ **Record the conclusion and
the evidence for it here** — that is what closes this row, not the register.

⚠️ **`New asset: Yes` is set on all three — and they are a 2014 truck, a 2017 van and a 2006 forklift.**
Used property can still take bonus where it is the taxpayer's **first** use and it came from an
unrelated party, so the outcome is probably unaffected; **but the flag as keyed asserts something the
assets are not. Check what that box drives in ATX before transmitting.**

⚠️ **The COST question is CLOSED for all three** — §2D's verification table matches every figure to the
ledger. 🔑 **The ProMaster's 10,000.00 IS the whole price**: 3,000.00 paid in cash on 04/14/2025 plus
7,000.00 financed through the `2017 RAM Loan`. ⛔ **What is NOT closed is whether the three costs include
sales tax, title and registration** — "cost" on a return is everything paid to put the asset in service.
The ledger holds what was paid to the seller. **Ask for the three invoices.**

|---|---|---|---|
| **①** | 🔴 **`Small tools and equipment` holds ONE line for the whole year: 07/01/2025, `QUANTUMTECHNOLOGY.NET`, 3,640.00** | **If that bought ONE piece of equipment it probably belongs on the 4562 as a fourth asset**, not in expense. If the invoice covers several smaller items it is properly expensed under the de minimis safe harbor, Reg. §1.263(a)-1(f) — **which then needs its own ELECTION STATEMENT attached to the return** (§3R). ⚠️ **The per-item threshold for a taxpayer with no applicable financial statement is not quoted here — irs.gov was unreachable; read it off the current instructions.** 🔴 **If it is capitalised: assets 30,340.00, page 1 line 22 falls to 10,005.18, and EACH shareholder's capital gain rises by 1,820.00** | 🛑 **YES — it moves figures** |
| **②** | ✅ **`2022 RAM 2500` — ASKED AND ANSWERED IN THIS PAPER. Nothing to put to anyone** | 🛑 **CORRECTED after review 2026-09-04, and the correction is the lesson.** ~~"Either a dead account, or the company owns a truck that was never put on the books"~~ — ⛔ **this paper already answers it twice**: §D reads the account at *"0.00 — disposed of in 2024, fully written off"*, and §E reads the **2024 filed return's own Form 4562**, which places the asset in service 1/30/2023, depreciates it, and shows Schedule L line 10a going to blank with accumulated depreciation to 0. ✅ **It is the dead leftover of a disposed asset**, which is also why an accumulated-depreciation account for it still sits in the chart. 🔑 **Putting it to Julia as an open question would have been the `look before you ask` failure** [`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) **rule 1 exists to stop** — the answer was two sections away | ⛔ **no, and do not ask** |
| **③** | 🟡 **The forklift's date in service is 08/25/2025 and matches neither payment** | the ledger shows **07/30/2025** 500.00 and **08/26/2025** 3,200.00. ⓘ **It changes not one dollar** — same quarter, same convention, 100% bonus. **Confirm it anyway so the register is right**; 08/25 for 08/26 is a plausible keying slip | no |

#### 🛠️ WHERE THE NUMBER GOES — and it depends on the election. This is the part that surprises people.

| Route | Where it is entered | Where it appears | What it reduces |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Plain MACRS** | Form 4562 Part III | **page 1 line 14** | ordinary business income (line 22) |
| **Bonus / special allowance** | Form 4562 line 14 | **page 1 line 14** | ordinary business income (line 22) |
| 🔴 **§179** | Form 4562 **Part I** | **Schedule K line 11** and **K-1 box 11** — ⛔ **NOT page 1 line 14** | **not** line 22. It is a **separately stated** deduction the shareholder takes on his own return |

🛑 **That last row is the one that catches people:** electing §179 leaves page 1 line 22 **unchanged at
40,345.18** and the deduction travels to the shareholder separately. **A preparer who expects line 22 to
drop and sees it unmoved will think the entry failed and key it twice.**

#### 📊 What each route does to the return — the seven figures that move

**Call the deduction `D`.** With MACRS or bonus:

| Figure | Formula | at D = 0 | at D = 5,340 *(5-yr, half-year, no bonus)* | at D = 26,700 *(full expensing)* |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Page 1 line 14 | `D` | 0 | 5,340.00 | 26,700.00 |
| Page 1 line 21 | `149,013.09 + D` | 149,013.09 | 154,353.09 | 175,713.09 |
| Page 1 line 22 | `40,345.18 − D` | **40,345.18** | **35,005.18** | **13,645.18** |
| Schedule K line 1 and line 18 | `= line 22` | 40,345.18 | 35,005.18 | 13,645.18 |
| Schedule L line 10b, accumulated depreciation | `D` *(if booked)* | 0 | (5,340.00) | (26,700.00) |
| Schedule L line 24, retained earnings | `(56,018.96) − D` | (56,018.96) | (61,358.96) | (82,718.96) |
| Schedule M-2 line 6 and line 8 | `(56,018.96) − D` | (56,018.96) | (61,358.96) | (82,718.96) |

⛔ **The 5,340 and 26,700 columns are ILLUSTRATIONS of the arithmetic, not recommendations** — 5,340 is
`26,700 × the first-year table rate for 5-year property, half-year convention`, and it assumes all three
assets are 5-year and none is caught by the mid-quarter convention. ⚠️ **The register says 5/5/7, not
5/5/5** *(§3H)*, so the real elect-out-of-bonus figure is **slightly lower than 5,340** — the forklift
runs on the 7-year table. ✅ **The mid-quarter half of the assumption is now CONFIRMED** — no Q4 asset. **The real figure comes out of ATX
once the dates are keyed.**
⚠️ **The rate itself is deliberately not written here, for the same reason as the bonus percentage** —
it is read off the MACRS table in the 2025 Form 4562 instructions, or produced by ATX. *(An earlier
version of this section wrote "20%" one paragraph after promising not to quote a rate from memory. The
figure happened to be right; **the promise still governs, because the next reader takes either the rule
or the number as licence.**)*

#### 🔴 AND THE PART THAT IS NOT ARITHMETIC — the election reaches the OWNERS' 1040s, and it does so DIFFERENTLY on each route

🛑 **CORRECTED after review.** An earlier version of this section said full expensing creates a capital
gain **whatever route it takes**. ⛔ **That is wrong for §179**, and the reason is the basis ordering
this paper states correctly in §3P: **income → distributions → nondeductible → losses and deductions.**
🔑 **§179 is a separately stated DEDUCTION — the LAST step — so it sits BEHIND the distributions and
cannot push them past his basis.** MACRS and bonus, by contrast, reduce **page 1 line 22**, which is the
*income* — an EARLY step — so they shrink the room the distributions have to fit into. **Same 26,700, two
completely different consequences.**
ⓘ **The step numbers below are §3P's table** *(0 opening · 1 contributions · **2 income** · 3
distributions · 4 nondeductible · **5 losses and deductions**)*. ⚠️ **Reg. §1.1367-1(f) numbers its own
ordering 1 to 4** — income, distributions, nondeductibles, losses — **so a "step 4" read off the
regulation is a "step 5" here.** 🛑 **An earlier version of this section mixed the two schemes and put
§179 on the nondeductibles row.**

| | **MACRS or bonus** *(page 1 line 14)* | **§179** *(Schedule K line 11)* |
|---|---|---|
| Basis step it lands on *(§3P's numbering)* | **step 2** — it reduces the income increase | **step 5** — after distributions **and** after the nondeductibles |
| Room for the distributions, **per shareholder** | `50,538.70 + (20,172.59 − D∕2)` | `50,538.70 + 20,172.59` — **unaffected by D** |
| 🔴 **A deduction above 21,358.55 in total** *(i.e. above 10,679.27 per half)* | **pushes each shareholder's distributions past his basis** — at D = 26,700 the entity gain of **5,341.45 splits: ~2,670.73 to Ishkhan and ~2,670.72 to Artem** *(less whatever opening basis each had, neither established)*. ⚠️ **Only ISHKHAN's is a final-year gain** | ✅ **no gain, for either, at any amount** |
| 🔴 **What happens instead** | the deduction is fully allowed | **§1366(d)(1) suspends whatever exceeds each shareholder's OWN remaining basis, separately.** At 26,700 split 13,350.00 each against 7,143.70 of basis each, **6,206.30 is disallowed for EACH of them — 12,412.60 in total** |
| 🛑 **And then — and this is where the two owners DIVERGE** | — | 🔴 **ISHKHAN's half dies. His K-1 is FINAL**, and a suspended loss is personal to the shareholder — on a complete disposition **his ~6,206.30 is permanently lost**, not carried to Artem and not deductible later. *(The §1366(d)(2)(B) exception is a transfer to a spouse or former spouse incident to divorce; a father-to-son transfer is not it.)* 🔑 **ONE LEVER SURVIVES, and it is worth naming: §1366(d)(3) lets a terminating shareholder deduct suspended losses during the POST-TERMINATION TRANSITION PERIOD, to the extent of his stock basis at the END of it — and basis can be restored by a CAPITAL CONTRIBUTION made during that period.** ⛔ **On these facts his ending basis is zero, so absent such a contribution it still dies** — but that is a planning decision someone can actually take, not a closed door. **Confirm the PTTP's length and its rules on the current instructions before relying on it** *(irs.gov unreachable from this session)*. *(Added in review 2026-09-04.)* ✅ **ARTEM's half does NOT die** — he continues into 2026, so his ~6,206.30 **suspends under §1366(d)(2) and carries forward** against future basis |

✅ **At D = 0 or at the small MACRS figure, neither problem arises on either route, for either owner.**
🛑 **So the election is not a timing choice here — each route carries a cost that "just expense it all"
would not anticipate.** ⚖️ **But the costs are NOT the same size, and saying they are would steer the
decision wrongly:** on **MACRS or bonus** each owner trades **13,350 of ordinary K-1 deduction** for
about **2,671 of LONG-TERM capital gain** *(Ishkhan has held the stock for years; **⚠️ Artem's holding
period depends on how and when he acquired the shares — on a §1015 gift it tacks under §1223(2), on a
purchase it does not**)* — **on most facts that is net favourable, not expensive.** On **§179** there is
no gain at all, but **~6,206 of deduction is destroyed for ISHKHAN** *(final K-1)* while **Artem's
merely suspends and carries into 2026.**
🔑 **Price all of it before electing, and price it PER OWNER — the two do not face the same trade.**
⛔ **Do not read this section as an argument for taking no depreciation.**
🛑 **CORRECTED after review** — an earlier version said the two routes "point the same way" and called a
large write-off the expensive choice **whichever door it came through.** ⛔ **That was wrong**, and it
pointed at electing nothing, which would forgo a deduction that is very likely worth more than the gain
it triggers.
⚠️ **Every figure here assumes an opening basis of ZERO for both of them** *(§3P step 0)* **and the
50/50 split of contributions and distributions, which is a working assumption.** **Any opening basis
either actually had reduces his suspension dollar for dollar** — which is why Ishkhan's 2024 Form 7203
line 15, and the size of Artem's basis at admission, both matter here and not only on their own returns.

#### ⚠️ 2026-09-05 — THE ATX ENTRY DOES **NOT** ANSWER IT, AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS BLOCK SAID IT DID

🛑 **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY, in review. What this block first said — *"the return now asserts that the
whole 18,986.63 is ARTEM's compensation and Ishkhan was paid nothing"* — is FALSE, and it was put to
Lilian as though it were settled.**

18,987, shareholder ticked, 100.00% of time devoted, 50.00% of common stock** — and Ishkhan is not on
it. **That is a fact about the keystroke, not a position on the return**, for two reasons:

   receives **one total on page 1 line 7** — no officer name, no per-officer amount, no percentage.
   **The return asserts nothing about the split.**
2. 🔑 **ATX's own instruction is to put the SUM on the FIRST ROW when the form is not required.** So a
   single row naming one person is **at least as consistent with following the software** as with a
   decision that the compensation is all his. **The first version of this block considered only the
   second reading.**

📌 **§3S row 0b therefore stays 🔴 OPEN.** The question — **whose compensation is the 18,986.63?** — is
still for Julia, and it is a question about **who worked**, which no software entry can answer.

Form 1040 — his FINAL year as a shareholder**, on the strength of something the return does not state.

#### 🛑 THE NOTE JULIA ASKED FOR — what was done, what it hides, and how to undo it

_(Lilian, 2026-09-05: *"siempre que hagas esto, haces la explicación en una nota… en caso de que no
queramos hacerlo así, nos demos cuenta de qué approach se tomó y lo corrijamos."* **This block is that
note.**)_

**① THE GROSS FIGURES — what any later analysis must use:**

| | Contributions | Distributions | Difference |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **Entity** | **101,077.40** *(50 rows)* | **101,077.40** *(after 18,986.63 moved to compensation)* | **0** |
| **Ishkhan** | 50,538.70 | 🔴 **60,032.02** | 🔴 **distributions exceed by 9,493.32** |
| **Artem** | 50,538.70 | **41,045.38** | contributions exceed by 9,493.32 |

**② WHICH OF §5C-v's FIVE GATES FAIL — three, and the first is the one that matters:**

| Gate | The condition | Here |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **1** | **That shareholder's** contributions exceed **their** distributions — *"one owner's contributions never offset another's distributions"* | ⛔ **FAILS FOR ISHKHAN** — his distributions exceed his contributions by 9,493.32. ✅ passes for Artem |
| **2** | No accumulated E&P | ✅ **passes** — zero at both ends of 2024 |
| **3** | AAA before distributions ≥ the distributions | ⛔ **fails** — the AAA is (101,705.59). ⓘ *neutral in practice: M-2 line 7 is capped at zero either way* |
| 🔴 **4** | The prior return ran contributions through **M-2 line 3** | ⛔ **FAILS** — the 2024 return did not (§3M) |
| **5** | The owner took no reasonable salary | ⚠️ **changed by decision 23** — there is now officer compensation |

**③ WHAT THE POLICY AS WRITTEN WOULD PRODUCE, versus what is reported:**

| | Schedule K 16d | Ishkhan box 16D | Artem box 16D |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **Gross** *(no netting)* | 101,077.40 | 60,032.02 | 41,045.38 |
| ⛔ ~~**§5C-v applied PER SHAREHOLDER** *(gate 1 as written)*~~ **— MISLABELLED, corrected 2026-09-05.** This row is **each account netted against itself**, which the policy does NOT authorise | **9,493.32** | **9,493.32** | **0** |
| ✅ **§5C-v ACTUALLY applied per shareholder** | **60,032.02** | 🔴 **60,032.02 — GROSS, because HIS gate 1 FAILS** | **0 — his passes** |
| 🔴 **AS REPORTED — netted at ENTITY level** | **0** | **0** | **0** |

🔑 **The difference between the entity row and the per-shareholder row is exactly gate 1:** entity-level netting offsets
**Artem's** 9,493.32 of excess contributions against **Ishkhan's** 9,493.32 of excess distributions.
**Gate 1 forbids that in terms.** ⚖️ **Reported this way on Julia's approval; this note exists so the
choice can be found and changed.**

**④ 🔴 WHAT IT DOES NOT DO — and this part has to reach the two 1040s:**

- 🛑 **It does NOT remove Ishkhan's capital gain.** §1368(b)(2) works on **actual** distributions
  against **actual** basis: 60,032.02 against 50,538.70 → **9,493.32 of gain on HIS OWN Form 1040**,
  ending stock basis zero, and his 2,670.73 loss suspended and **lost** on a final K-1. ⛔ **His K-1
  reads box 16D = 0 and mentions none of it** (§8C).
- ~~⚠️ **BUT that gain rests on an opening basis of ZERO, which this paper says is FALSE for him**~~
  🟢 **CORRECTED 2026-09-05: the 2024 Form 7203 arrived and LINE 15 IS ZERO** *(§3P-2)*. **The opening
  basis really is zero and the gain stands.** 🔴 **But the same form carries 48,125 of SUSPENDED LOSSES
  into 2025**, and on a netted box 16D a preparer of his 1040 would deduct 47,003.13 of them instead.
  🛑 **§3P-2 ③ and §3S row 0c — this is now the biggest single figure the netting decision moves.**
- ✅ **Artem is unaffected:** 50,538.70 against 41,045.38 leaves **9,493.32 of room**, his 3,535.57 of
  nondeductibles are absorbed, **his 2,670.72 loss is FULLY DEDUCTIBLE in 2025**, and **3,287.03** of
  basis carries into 2026.
- ⚠️ **It removes the marker for a non-pro-rata distribution** — two K-1s both reading zero look
  identical when one owner drew 60,032.02 and the other 41,045.38. The visible evidence of a possible
  **second class of stock** (§1361(b)(1)(D)) leaves the face of the return; the fact does not.
- 📌 **A later reasonable-compensation study reading distributions off box 16D will read ZERO.**
  🔑 **On this client, distributions come from the capital-account ledger, never from the return.**

**⑤ HOW TO UNDO IT.** Nothing in the books changes — the netting is presentation only. **Restore
Schedule K 16d to 101,077.40 and box 16D to 60,032.02 / 41,045.38**, and **no other line moves.**
✅ **That is the whole reversal.**

#### 🛑 THE APIC RULE — do NOT compute line 23 independently

⛔ **AND THIS ALSO SUPERSEDES §3H-4'S ASIDE that APIC *"still presents as 89,669"*.** ⛔ **The instruction this paper gave — *"APIC = 88,464 + contributions + 1,204.12 − distributions,

lands on (101,704), and the balance sheet then misses by 2. 🔴 **Lilian hit exactly that.**

         =  89,667

#### ⚠️ Three things about the equity lines, and each needs saying

**① The rounding.** The roll-forward gives APIC **70,681.49** and the balance sheet needs **70,682** for
the two sides to agree in whole dollars. **The 0.06 comes from the 2024 return, which was filed in whole
dollars against books carried in cents.** 🔑 **A rounding difference belongs on the aggregated line —
here, paid-in capital — never on a line that represents a real, traceable balance.**

**② The AAA and retained earnings landing on the same figure is a COINCIDENCE, not a rule.**
🔴 **The firm's own SOP says so in red** *(`form-1120s-preparation.md` §10C)*: *"The AAA is a **TAX**
number and retained earnings is a **BOOK** number. They are not the same thing and they are not supposed
to agree."* **Three things make them coincide this year and none of them will recur reliably:** M-2 line
7 is **zero** *(the AAA is negative)*; the distributions run through **paid-in capital**, not through
retained earnings, under the inherited 2024 shape; and the 2024 return happened to end both lines on the
same rounded figure. 🛑 **Give this company a positive AAA next year and line 8 falls by the
distributions while line 24 does not.** ⛔ **So do not carry "M-2 line 8 = L line 24" forward as a check
— a preparer taught that rule will PLUG a correct return to make it hold.** 🛠️ **What actually reconciles
them is the software's bridge column** — SOP §10C has the opening-balance formula.

**③ The presentation itself is inherited, and it is unusual.** 📄 **The 2024 return folded the Opening
Balance Equity, the contributions, the distributions and `Owner's Equity` into ONE paid-in-capital
line**, leaving retained earnings to carry only the accumulated result. **The tables above continue
that**, which is why line 23 falls this year: the distributions run through it.
⚖️ **The alternative — running distributions through retained earnings instead — gives APIC 189,745.52
and retained earnings (175,082.99).** **Total equity is 14,662.53 either way**, and the K-1, the AAA
and every tax figure are untouched. 🔑 **Continuity with the filed 2024 column is why the inherited
shape wins** — a reviewer comparing the two years should see one column continue into the next.

#### 🔴 THREE THINGS THIS OPENS, AND THE FIRST ONE CAN CHANGE BOTH K-1s

**1 · 🛑 A FLAT 50% IS ONLY RIGHT IF ARTEM HELD 50% FROM 1 JANUARY 2025.**
2024 closed with **Ishkhan at 100 of 100 shares** (§E-1). So Artem was admitted **at some point**, and
**when** decides the arithmetic:
- ✅ **Admitted 1 January 2025** *(or the transfer effective at the close of 12/31/2024)* → **a flat
  50/50 for the whole year, which is what these tables assume.**
- 🔴 **Admitted DURING 2025** → **§1377(a)(1) requires a PER-DAY allocation**, and the flat 50%
  misstates **both** K-1s. ⓘ **§1377(a)(1) is the default in every S year** — with constant ownership
  it simply collapses to a percentage; a mid-year admission is where it stops collapsing. ⛔ **The
  closing-of-the-books election (§1377(a)(2)) is NOT available for an admission** — it requires a
  shareholder to terminate his *entire* interest.
- ⚠️ **Separately, a *qualifying disposition* under Reg. §1.1368-1(g) may be in play, and the paper's
  earlier note about it was both incomplete and self-contradictory. Corrected:**
  **The definition has more than one prong**, and the one that fits a father transferring shares to his
  son is **a disposition by a shareholder of 20% or more of the outstanding stock in any 30-day period**
  — *not* only the issuance prong *(25% or more issued to new shareholders in 30 days)* the earlier note
  gave. ⛔ **Reading only the issuance prong makes the election look unavailable when it may well be
  available.**
  🛑 **AND WHAT THE ELECTION REACHES IS NOT SETTLED HERE.** The earlier note said it splits the year for
  **§1368 / AAA** purposes *"only, never for income"* — **and then called it "not academic" because the
  AAA is negative, which cannot both be true**: if it is AAA-only, and the AAA is negative all year with
  no accumulated E&P, **it changes nothing** *(M-2 line 7 is zero either way — §3M, and taxability turns
  on basis under §1368(b)(2), which an AAA election does not reach)*. ⚠️ **The Instructions for Form
  1120-S describe this election under *Allocation of items to shareholders* in terms of treating the
  year as two tax years, which reads wider than AAA-only.** ⛔ **This session could not pull irs.gov to
  settle it, so it is flagged rather than asserted.** 🛠️ **Read Reg. §1.1368-1(g) and the 2025
  instructions before relying on either reading.**
🛠️ **ASK JULIA FOR THE DATE.** It is one line in her notes and it decides whether these two K-1s are
right.

**2 · ⚠️ THE 50/50 DISTRIBUTION SPLIT IS AN ASSUMPTION LILIAN MADE TO GET MOVING, NOT A FINDING.**
Her words: *"vamos por ahora a asumir… y luego que Julia determine cómo lo vamos a resolver."*
🔑 **Income allocation follows OWNERSHIP; distributions are reported AS ACTUALLY MADE.** They are two
different determinations and only the first is settled. **The ledger holds 94 distribution rows for
2025 and does not say whose each one is** — the method for splitting them for real is §3P below.
🔴 **AND ONE THING TO WATCH WHILE IT IS OPEN:** an S corporation may have **only one class of stock**
(§1361(b)(1)(D)), and materially disproportionate distributions between two 50% owners are evidence of
a second. ⚖️ **The regulation's test is the GOVERNING PROVISIONS, not the amounts** *(Reg.
§1.1361-1(l)(2)(i))*, so timing differences are ordinarily fine — **but a large, sustained imbalance is
not a timing difference.** ⛔ **Do not characterise it; record it for Julia** *(§D-9)*.

**3 · 🔴 IS ARTEM AN ELIGIBLE S-CORPORATION SHAREHOLDER? NOBODY HAS ASKED, AND IT OUTRANKS EVERYTHING
ELSE ON THE OPEN LIST.**
🔑 **The reason the ownership changed at all, on this file's own record, is the shareholder-eligibility
rule:** §E-1 states it — *"a nonresident shareholder is disqualifying, so he left ownership"*, and
**§1361(b)(1)(C) says a nonresident alien may not hold S-corporation stock at all.** ⛔ **A second 50%
shareholder has now been added to the year and his status has never been raised.** 🛑 **An ineligible
shareholder TERMINATES the S election** — which would not change a line on this return, it would change
**which return this is.** ⚖️ **Lilian closed the equivalent question about Ishkhan on 2026-09-02**
*("Asume que no se perdió el estatus de S-Corporation y que todo está en orden en ese sentido")* — ⚠️
**read precisely, that is an assurance about the OUTCOME, and it was given when Artem was not thought to
be a 2025 shareholder.** 🛠️ **Put it to Julia in the same breath as the admission date: is Artem a US
person for §1361 purposes, and was he on the day he was admitted?**

**4 · ⚠️ IT SITS AWKWARDLY WITH §4 DECISION 1, AND THAT IS WORTH SAYING OUT LOUD.**
Julia ruled that **the whole Turo relationship is ARTEM's**. The 3,341.08 reclassified by
`JE 2025-ADJ 3` is European-car parts, the parts catalogue, Turo rentals and rental insurance — **on

  is Masciave's problem, not a feature of the technique.**
- **Masciave's 2025 K-1 is marked FINAL.** Ecoorganic's final K-1 is Ishkhan's, for unrelated reasons.
