### D · 🟡 Turo — THE FIGURES ARRIVED 2026-09-01. What is left is ONE question: whose income is it?

🔑 **The client received Turo's answer and Lilian supplied it on 2026-09-01.** It is an email from
Turo Executive Support to Artem. **It was not in the repo before this** — every Turo record stopped at
2026-08-04 — so the "waiting on Turo" account further down is now history, kept because it explains
how the figures were obtained.

**What Turo reported for 2025 — host earnings:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Gross earnings** | **10,800.78** |
| less **Turo fee** | **(2,601.19)** |
| **= Trip earnings** | **8,199.59** |
| plus **Reimbursements** | **628.15** |
| **= net cash reaching the host** | **8,827.74** |

✅ **The email is internally consistent** — `10,800.78 − 2,601.19 = 8,199.59` exactly, and the fee is
**24.1%** of gross, a normal Turo host take-rate. **Reimbursements are listed SEPARATELY and are
therefore NOT inside the 10,800.78.**

🛑 **NO FORM 1099-K WAS ISSUED FOR 2025.** Turo states the host *"did not qualify… as your host
earnings did not meet the IRS filing threshold of $20,000+ in gross earnings and 200+ transactions."*
⚠️ **Three things follow, and the second is the one that gets forgotten:**
- **The income is taxable whether or not a 1099-K exists.** The absence of the form changes nothing
  about reporting it.
- 🔴 **The corroboration this paper was waiting for is now GONE PERMANENTLY.** The plan — and
  `FOLLOW-UPS` 15 — was to read **the name and TIN on the 1099-K** to establish whether the account
  belongs to the LLC or to Artem personally. **There is no 1099-K, so that route does not exist.**
  Ownership is now settled by asking, or from the Turo account registration — nothing else.
- ✅ **TURO'S THRESHOLD IS CORRECT — verified against irs.gov on 2026-09-01**, not quoted from memory.
  *Understanding your Form 1099-K* (page last reviewed **28 June 2026**) states: **"Third party
  settlement organizations (TPSOs) (payment apps and online marketplaces) are required to report
  payments on Form 1099-K when the total amount of payments you receive for goods or services through
  the platform exceeds $20,000 in more than 200 transactions."** ⚠️ **The page states the rule without
  breaking it out by year**, so a year-specific question would still need that year's instructions —
  but on the current wording Turo applied it correctly and no 1099-K was due.
  ⓘ **The page also notes** that a taxpayer *"may receive a Form 1099-K even when total payments or
  transactions are less than the reporting threshold"* — Turo says it issued none, and Turo is the filer.

**Both figures are right.** D-2 answers *"what did this company spend with European-car vendors?"*;
D-4 answers *"what is inside the vehicle-repair account?"* **Use D-4 for the reclassification, because
that is the account that gets adjusted.**

##### §3 · CheetahWrap 530.69 — **can we tell what state? Answer: NO, and here is exactly why**

**What the ledger holds is the bank descriptor and nothing else: `SP CHEETAHWRAP`, 2025-05-26, 530.69,
Chase credit card.** There is no location field on a transaction in Double or QuickBooks — the only
geography a bank feed ever carries is whatever the merchant puts in the descriptor.

🔑 **And here the descriptor tells us something better than a state: `SP ` is Shop Pay** — Shopify's
checkout. **This was an ONLINE ORDER, not a visit to a shop.** So "which state was it incurred in" has
no answer of the kind the question expects: it was bought from a website and **shipped somewhere**.
⚠️ *(The reading of `SP ` is from the descriptor convention — this same ledger writes Square as
`SQ *BUEN GUSTO RESTAURANT` and Shop Pay as `SP UROTUNING.COM`. **Inferred from the pattern, not
confirmed with the merchant.**)*

✅ **But the state question is still answerable — from the SHIPPING ADDRESS on the order.** That lives
in the CheetahWrap order confirmation, in whichever mailbox placed the order.
⛔ **Not in Julia's:** a Gmail search for `CheetahWrap OR "cheetah wrap"` on 2026-09-01 returned
**nothing at all**. **So it has to be asked of the client** — and it is a good thing to ask, because a
wrap shipped to Florida and a wrap shipped to the Darien shop are different answers.

##### ✅ AND THE SAME QUESTION *DOES* HAVE AN ANSWER FOR OTHER CHARGES — which is the useful half

**Some descriptors in this account carry the city and state outright**, and every one of them is a
**debit-card charge on the Chase operating account**, not a credit-card charge:

| The descriptor, with its trailing reference tokens stripped | What it gives us |
|---|---|
| `AUTOZONE 1887 9858 M SHIPPENSBURG PA … 08/04` | **Shippensburg, Pennsylvania** |
| `AUTOZONE 5156 50 CROS NORWALK CT … 09/30` | **Norwalk, Connecticut** |
| `LOSTOCCO AUTO … RIDGEFIELD CT 10/03` | **Ridgefield, Connecticut** |
| `EXXON DARIEN TPKE (E)` | **Darien, Connecticut** — the shop's own town |

⛔ **The `…` is deliberate.** Each of these descriptors ends in a masked reference token of the
`XX####` shape. **We do not know what those digits are** — a card, a terminal, a truncated merchant
phone — and [`projects/tax-returns/README.md`](../README.md) says that if you cannot tell which a
figure is, it does not go in. **The city and state are the whole point here; the token adds nothing.**

🔑 **So the rule for this client is: the debit-card feed carries the location, the credit-card feed does
not.** That is worth knowing for every future question of this shape. **For a credit-card charge the
only route to a location is the merchant's own receipt or the card statement, from the client.**

##### §4 · The Zelle payments to individuals — **why I called them mechanics, and why that was wrong**

**Lilian's question, verbatim:** *"¿Por qué sabes que las transferencias vía Zelle a particulares, como
en Emanuel, Adam, Lado, son mecánicos? ¿Cómo sabes eso? Por la categorización que tienen? porque
pudiera ser también que fuesen subcontractors."*

🛑 **The honest answer is: yes, only the categorisation. Nothing else.** Emanuel, Adam and Lado were
called mechanics **because the bookkeeper posted them to `Vehicles repair, supplies and others`** — a
vehicle account. There is no invoice, no W-9, no memo, no note, and no other evidence anywhere in the
sources read. ⛔ **That is a restatement of someone's categorisation dressed up as a finding, and it is
exactly the failure [`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) rule 1b names.**

**What the ledger DOES support, and it is a real distinction:**

| | Posted to | Who is in it |
|---|---|---|
| The people called "mechanics" | `Vehicles repair…` (43) | Adam · Emanuel · Lado — small, sporadic, **1,840.00** for the year |
| The people the books treat as **labour** | `Outside services` (1150040005) | Kozami LLC/Sviat Kozak · Bryant Torres · TIGASI Home Improvement · Nikita Avdeev · Cristian Amaya Escobar · Steven Gomes · Cesar Nahuis · Ariel Alex Cardona Contreras · Andrzej Kiszkiel — recurring, four-figure |

🔴 **AND ONE OF THE THREE CROSSES THE LINE. `Emanuel` (vendor 279) was paid 600.00 into `Vehicles
repair` on 2025-04-15. `ENMANUEL GUABA-VASQUEZ` (vendor 473) was paid 400.00 into `Outside services`
on 2025-12-17.** Two vendor records, one very probably the same person, **filed as a car repair in
April and as labour in December.** ⚠️ **Same-person is an inference from the name, not established** —
but if it holds, Lilian's reading is the right one and this is a **subcontractor**, which means a
**Form 1099-NEC question**, not a vehicle question.

**What settles it:** ask Artem who Adam, Emanuel and Lado are. It is one message, and it decides both
the account and whether a 1099 was owed. ⚖️ **2026-09-02: the ACCOUNT half is closed by Lilian (§4 decision 8 — auto expense, not asked); only the Schedule B 14a half remains.**

##### §5 · TWO THINGS THIS ANALYSIS ADDS THAT THE ACCOUNT ITSELF DOES NOT SHOW

🟡 **① `Partslink24` sits in `Software`, not in any vehicle account — and that is the new part, not the
vendor.** ⚠️ **The vendor is NOT a new find:** Partslink24 175.00 is already in D-2's German-marque list
and in D-old, and the bridge above says in terms that D-2's 9,323.81 **includes** it. **What this pass
adds is where it lives.** Five charges of 35.00 (2025-04-21, 05-18, 06-17, 07-17, 08-18) posted to
`Software` (46). **Partslink24 is the OEM parts catalogue for European marques**, so a subscription to it
is hard to place in a spray-foam business — and because it is filed under `Software`, **a review of the
car accounts (including this one) does not see it, and the reclassification would leave it behind.**
⚠️ *(What Partslink24 is, is stated from general knowledge of the vendor — it was not verified against
the vendor's own site in this session, which has no access to it.)*

🔴 **② A 1,087.94 REFUND IS CREDITED AGAINST THE WRONG ACCOUNT.** `AUDI JAX`, 2025-06-05, 1,087.94 —
credited to **`Vehicle gas and fuel` (42)**, not to `Vehicles repair…` (43) where the original charges
sit. It is **the only credit posted to the fuel account in the whole of 2025.** Two consequences:
- `Vehicles repair…` is **overstated by 1,087.94** and the real fuel figure is **6,702.70**, not 5,614.76.
- It explains a discrepancy this paper already carried: the expenses-by-vendor report shows Audi
  Jacksonville at **1,025.55** while account 43 holds **2,113.49** — the report nets across accounts,
  the account does not. **Both figures were right; they were measuring different things.**

✅ **Net income does not move** — it is a transfer between two expense accounts. **But it moves the
personal-vs-business split**, because all of the Audi Jacksonville activity is Florida.

##### 🛠️ §5b · HOW TO FIX BOTH — step by step, because a finding without its fix is half a finding

**Asked by Lilian, 2026-09-02.** 🔑 **The two are NOT the same kind of problem, and that decides how
each is fixed:**

| | What it is | So it is fixed |
|---|---|---|
| **The 1,087.94 refund** | a **MISCODING** — the money is in the wrong account and always was | **at source** — recategorise the transaction itself, so the register shows the truth |
| **Partslink24 175.00** | a **DECISION** — the coding is defensible, the treatment is a judgement | **with a year-end journal entry**, alongside the rest of its block |

⛔ **Do not fix a decision with a recategorisation, or an error with a journal entry.** A JE over a
miscoding hides what actually happened; a recategorisation of a judgement scatters one decision across
the year and makes it impossible to see later.

---

**🔧 FIX 1 — the Audi Jacksonville refund. One field on one transaction.**

**Where it is:** QuickBooks → *Reports* → **Transaction Detail by Account** → `Auto Expenses:Vehicle
gas and fuel`, dates 2025-01-01 to 2025-12-31. **It is the only credit in that account all year**, so
it is the one line with a figure in the wrong direction. *(Or open the Chase credit-card register and
go to 2025-06-05.)*

**The transaction:** `AUDI JAX` · **2025-06-05** · **1,087.94** · type **Credit Card Credit** · vendor
**Audi Jacksonville**.

**What to change — ONE field:**

| Field | From | To |
|---|---|---|
| **Category / Account** | `Auto Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel` | `Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair, supplies and others` |

⛔ **Change nothing else.** Not the date, not the amount, not the vendor, not the payment account.

**How to know it worked — four checks, and all four must hold:**

**Read every figure below off the 2025 P&L in QuickBooks — the same report, before and after.**
⚠️ *Not off the D-4 rebuild: that rebuild gives 6,636.88 for the repair account while the P&L shows
6,839.99, and the 203.11 between them is still unresolved (D-1d). A check table has to use one basis,
and the one you will actually be looking at is the P&L.*

| On the 2025 P&L | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| `Auto Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel` | 5,614.76 | **6,702.70** |
| `Auto Expenses:Vehicles repair, supplies and others` | 6,839.99 | **5,752.05** |
| `Auto Expenses` **parent** total | 15,136.25 | **15,136.25 — unchanged** |
| **Net income** | — | **unchanged** |

✅ **The parent total not moving is the check that matters** — both accounts are children of
`Auto Expenses`, so a correct fix cannot move it. **If the parent moves, something else was edited.**
ⓘ *If the repair line lands at 5,548.94 instead of 5,752.05, the 203.11 gap has resolved itself in the
meantime — that is information, not a failure; note it in D-1d.*
✅ And the expenses-by-vendor report for Audi Jacksonville **stays at 1,025.55**, because that report
already netted across accounts — it was right all along, which is how this was found.

🛑 **DO THIS BEFORE THE TURO / PERSONAL RECLASSIFICATION, NOT AFTER.** If the Audi charges are moved out
of `Vehicles repair…` first, **the 1,087.94 credit is left stranded in the fuel account** — a refund on
a car the company does not own, permanently understating fuel, and nobody will ever find it again.

✅ **It is safe to make even though 2025 is closed and a 2025 P&L has already been supplied for the
insurance premium audit** *(the client file §5 records the audit asking for the P&L and a contractor
list, from Gmail — D-2b ⑥ carries the same caveat)*: **net income and the `Auto Expenses` parent do not
move. The two child lines DO move**, by 1,087.94 each, in opposite directions — so if a line-level P&L
was what went out, those two lines now differ from it, while every total on it is unchanged.

⚠️ **2025 is treated as closed in these books** (the August 2026 cleanup was applied to 2026 only, by
convention). **If a closing date is set in QuickBooks, this edit will challenge for the closing-date
password.** Going past it is a **deliberate exception** — the 2025 return is not filed yet, so the books
it is prepared from should be right — and it is **Julia's go-ahead, not a preparer's.**

---

**🔧 FIX 2 — Partslink24. `Owner's distribution` is the LIKELY treatment — and two conditions come BEFORE it can be posted.**

**Lilian's question:** *"¿Cómo puedo corregirlo? ¿Lo re categorizo a owner's distribution?"*
**The instinct is right, and here is what supports it:** the company's entire fleet is a **2014 Isuzu
NPR**, a **2017 RAM ProMaster** and a **2006 Nissan forklift**. ⛔ **Not one of them is a European
marque.** A subscription to a European-marque OEM parts catalogue cannot be serving that fleet.

✅ **CONDITION 1 — DONE 2026-09-02: Lilian opened the site; the catalogue covers 24 entries and none of them is an Isuzu, a RAM or a Nissan (§D-8 A-2).** ~~🛑 CONDITION 1 — confirm what the subscription actually is, in a browser, before moving anything.~~
⚠️ What Partslink24 is, is stated here **from general knowledge of the vendor; this session had no
access to check it.** It is a ten-second look. **If it turns out the business genuinely uses it, none of
the above applies.**

🛑 **CONDITION 2 — it follows the Audi/BMW block; it is NOT decided on its own.** This is Turo-related
spending by the same reasoning as the rest, so **Julia's decision on that block governs it.** If any of
the block stays a business expense, Partslink24 stays with it. ⛔ **Splitting it off and deciding it
separately is the trap** — it is the smallest item and the easiest to get inconsistent.

**Where they are:** QuickBooks → *Expenses* → filter by vendor **Partslink24**, 2025. **Five charges of
35.00**: 2025-04-21 · 05-18 · 06-17 · 07-17 · 08-18, all on the Chase credit card, all coded `Software`.

**How to make the change — follow Julia's own precedent, one year-end entry:**

```
Journal entry, dated 2025-12-31
   Dr   Owner's distribution (49)        175.00
        Cr   Software (46)                       175.00
   Memo: Partslink24 2025 — European-marque parts catalogue — distribution to
         ARTEM MARKARIAN — reclassified with the vehicle block per Julia's
         decision of <date of her ruling on the block>
```

🔴 **NAME THE SHAREHOLDER IN THE MEMO, every time.** This company has **one pooled distribution
account** — 🛑 **written when this paper believed there were two shareholders; SUPERSEDED, see blocker B: there is ONE, and nothing to split** — and any per-owner split **does not exist anywhere in the ledger**
(blocker B). Julia's Turo ruling makes this a distribution **for Artem's benefit — and on Julia's date the shareholder it is a distribution TO is ISHKHAN (§B-0, §B-1)** — so a line posted to the pooled
account with no name is **untestable** against gate 1 of the netting policy *(which is per shareholder)*
and against his Form 7203. The memo is the only place the attribution survives.
🛑 **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-02 — do NOT interrupt Julia with this.** This bullet raised a
**disproportionate-distribution / single-class-of-stock** question, which can only arise with **two or
more** shareholders. **There is one** (blocker B). ⛔ **The concern does not exist. The memo should still
name Artem**, because the pooled account is shared with prior years and the attribution has to survive.

🔑 **Why a journal entry and not five recategorisations.** ① It is **exactly what `JournalEntry-2025-ADJ`
already did** for the same kind of item — it moved auto costs to `Owner's distribution` at 2025-12-31
*(Julia's workbook documents that entry; who posted it is not recorded)*. ② It **leaves the original coding intact**, so a year from now you can still see what was
bought, from whom and when. ③ **One auditable entry** instead of five edits scattered across the year.
**Put it in the SAME entry as the rest of the block** when Julia rules on it — one decision, one entry.
⚠️ **Same closing-date note as fix 1:** a 2025-12-31 entry in a closed year will challenge for the
closing-date password if one is set. Deliberate exception, Julia's go-ahead.

**How to know it worked:**

| | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| `Software` (46), 2025 P&L | *(current balance)* | **less 175.00** |
| **FY2025 MOVEMENT** in `Owner's distribution` (49) — *not the balance-sheet balance, which is a running total* | 115,722.95 | **115,897.95** |
| **Net income** | — | **up 175.00** — an expense was removed |

⚠️ **Read the distribution line as the 2025 movement** — an equity report or the account register
filtered to 2025, not the balance sheet. **The balance sheet shows the cumulative balance (§3 records
it at (137,814.68) at 2025-12-31) and will not match the row above.**

⚠️ **AND THIS ONE DOES MOVE THE RETURN, unlike fix 1.** Net income rises, so **the K-1 moves** *(one K-1 since 2026-09-02)*, and
**distributions rise**. 🛑 **Distributions are already 115,722.95 and that is the exact figure the basis
test in blocker B runs on.** 175.00 is trivial; **the Audi block behind it is not**, and every dollar
moved from expense to distribution pushes that number further above the contributions. **Read blocker B
before the block entry is posted, not after.**
🔁 **CORRECTED 2026-09-02 — the move is BASIS-NEUTRAL, so the warning above is withdrawn.** Every dollar
moved from expense to distribution raises ordinary income by that dollar *(basis up, §1367(a)(1))* and
distributions by the same dollar *(basis down, §1367(a)(2))*: **the shareholder's ending basis does not move
at all** *(the 2025 shareholder is ISHKHAN — §B-0; §B-1 is closed on reading ①)*, and the no-capital-gain conclusion in blocker B survives the whole of D-6. What moves is
presentation — page 1, line 16d, the AAA — not the tax.

##### 📗 D-6b · THE 7,456.41 EXPLAINED — what Julia's workbook is, what it says about it, and what it cannot say

**Lilian's question, 2026-09-02:** *"explícame mejor ese monto de $7,456.41. Dime ese worksheet que Julia
tiene creado en el Drive. ¿Qué relación tiene con este monto?… dame el link."*

**The document:** `ECOORGANIC_2025_PL_Review_Transactions.xlsx` —
[open it in Drive](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1NxRxYsvHt-TfyeJ9ESbfF4cldfzUqbld/view) *(owner
Julia, created 2026-07-23, last modified the same day; folder id `1hI_nOpt6Vx8JCSm71M3Ivm-9s7pmuGQd`)*.
✅ Read on 2026-09-01 with Lilian's authorisation and re-read on 2026-09-02 to answer this.

**Step by step — what it is and how the 7,456.41 sits in it:**

1. **What Julia was doing.** On 2026-07-23 she ran a review of the 2025 P&L *"from live QuickBooks via
   Double"*, listing transaction by transaction the rows that looked wrong, each with a **flag** (RED =
   likely miscategorised or personal, YELLOW = confirm/reclassify, OK = fine or a refund), the **account
   it sits in**, date, vendor, amount and a **suggested action**. Seven accounts were listed; `Meals` and
   `Travel:Hotel` were left for later (*"Ask me to add them"*).
2. **The vehicle-repair account is the biggest block in it: 22 RED rows.** They are mostly the
   European-car charges D-4 §1 identifies *(the workbook groups rows differently from D-4's ledger rebuild,
   so the two counts do not map one to one)* — FCP Euro, UroTuning, Audi Jacksonville, Audi North Miami, Audi
   Greenwich, Audi Fairfield, Audi Parts Store, BMW Service Miami — each with the same note: *"Luxury /
   European performance-car spend; company fleet is RAM trucks / ProMaster / Isuzu. Likely owner's personal
   vehicle — confirm business use or move to Owner's draw."* Plus Valvoline, Emanuel, Adam, CheetahWrap in
   RED; Just Four Tires, H.O. Penn, Adam, Lado, the 6.64 Exxon in YELLOW; the refunds and AutoZone/NAPA
   as OK.
3. **The 7,456.41 appears in that same account as ONE row, dated 2025-12-31, flagged OK:** vendor
   *"Year-end JE (adj)"*, description *"JE: credit Vehicle repair → Owner's distribution"*, amount
   **(7,456.41)**, note **"Accountant already reclassified $7,456.41 of vehicle costs to Owner's
   distribution. Confirms personal-auto issue."** And in the *CONTEXT* box at the end: *"Year-end adjusting

   (Vehicle repair) + $1,804.12 (Opening Balance Equity), and reclassed a 2017 RAM ProMaster $7,000
   asset/loan. This confirms significant personal-auto activity."*

   **and it found that somebody had ALREADY moved 7,456.41 of that account to distributions before the
   review** — it records the entry as a fact it met, not as something the review created. 🛑 **It does

   beside it. The account's net in the workbook, 6,636.88, is the gross charges less the refunds less
   that entry — the same arithmetic as D-4.
5. **Why the ledger cannot decompose it either:** the entry is one credit of 7,456.41 to the account with
   no reference to transactions. Tested 2026-09-02: **no combination of the account's vendor totals sums
   to 7,456.41** *(and at the level of individual charges there are hundreds of thousands of combinations,
   which is the same as none)*.

##### 🧾 D-6c · THE JOURNAL ENTRY — account by account, which side each goes on, and what it is FOR

🛑 **THE AMOUNTS BELOW ARE SUPERSEDED BY §D-8** *(2026-09-02, evening — the entry is 3,341.08, not 4,533.32)*. ✅ **What is still current here is the THEORY** — which account is what kind, why the debit and the credits go where they do, and what each schedule of the return sees afterwards.

**Lilian's question:** *"¿cuál sería exactamente el journal entry que tengo que introducir? ¿Me puedes
explicar las cuentas que van a estar relacionadas, cuáles se van a debitar y cuáles se van a acreditar?…
cuéntame qué es el objetivo de esto."*

🔴 **On Julia's date (§B-0) the shareholder is ISHKHAN.** The distribution lands on **his** K-1 box 16D and
**his** Form 7203, and it is a **constructive distribution** — the company paid the shareholder's son's car
costs — followed by a gift to Artem. ⚠️ **If Julia takes reading ② of §B-1** (the money is Artem's:
compensation, or a loan account), **the CREDIT lines of this entry stay exactly as they are and only the
DEBIT account changes** — compensation, or a due-to-Artem liability, instead of `Owner's distribution`.
**Post nothing until she has chosen.** The paragraphs below keep "the shareholder" generic for that reason.

**The objective, in one sentence:** the company's return may deduct only the company's costs; the money
the company spent on Artem's cars in 2025 is **not lost, it is money the company handed to its shareholder**
— so it comes **out of the expense accounts** and **into `Owner's distribution`**, where the return reports
it as a distribution to him (Schedule K line 16d, his K-1 box 16 code D, his Form 7203), and **on his side
the Turo-related part becomes his own Schedule C deduction** (§8A) ⛔ **— narrowed by §D-8 tier B: moving the company's own Turo RENTALS creates no Schedule C deduction, because renting a car from Turo is not a cost of hosting cars on it.** One entry, two returns kept honest.

**Why these two sides, for someone who wants the theory and not the jargon:**

| The account | What kind it is | Its normal balance | What the entry does to it | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Owner's distribution` (49) | **Equity** — a "contra-equity" account that counts what the owner has taken out | **debit** (debits make it bigger) | **DEBIT** — it grows by the total | value left the company and went to the shareholder. It is not an expense of the business and never reaches the P&L; it reduces his basis instead |
| `Vehicles repair…` (43) · `Software` (46) · *(tier 2)* `Taxi or shared rides` (45) · `Auto insurance` (50) · `Parking` (58) · `Vehicle gas and fuel` (42) | **Expense** accounts | **debit** (a charge posted to them is a debit) | **CREDIT** — each shrinks by its line | a credit to an expense account is the opposite of a charge: it takes cost out. The P&L shows less expense, so **net income rises by exactly the total** |

**Debits = credits, always.** The one debit line equals the sum of the credit lines. Nothing touches cash
or the credit cards — the money was already spent in 2025; the entry only re-labels **who it was spent
for**. On the balance sheet total equity does not move: net income rises by X *(retained earnings up X)*
and distributions rise by X *(equity down X)* — only the composition changes, which is the point.

**The entry itself** — dated **2025-12-31**, posted ONCE after Julia's two answers (D-6 STEP 2):

```

  #  ACCOUNT                                        DEBITS      CREDITS

     and others
  3  Software                                                     175.00

  6  Auto Expenses:Parking                                        107.03
  7  Auto Expenses:Vehicle gas and fuel                           453.86
                                        TOTAL      4,533.32     4,533.32
```

2026-09-01 that the Turo host activity and its vehicles are personal, not the
company's. Personal costs paid by an S corporation for its shareholder are
distributions, not deductible expenses (IRC 262; 1368).

TO WHOM: Ishkhan Markarian, the sole shareholder for the whole of 2025 (he holds
100% for all 365 days; the transfer to Artem Markarian is effective at the close
of 12/31/2025). Payments that benefited Artem are the shareholder's own money
spent for his son and are NOT re-characterised.

HOW THE AMOUNTS WERE BUILT (working paper section D-6d, from the 2025 General
Ledger):
 - Vehicles repair 1,692.40 = the European-marque charges left in the account
   (10,236.75 gross) less the 7,456.41 already moved by JE 2025-ADJ on 12/31/2025

EFFECT: no cash moves. Deductible expenses fall and ordinary income rises by

expense account is identical on both bases.**
✅ **ANSWERED 2026-09-02 (evening): the 2024 return was filed on the CASH basis, so 2025 follows it** —

receivable.**

carries a MARCH trip date"*. **It does not** — the descriptors read `TRIP FEB 8`, `TRIP FEB 10`, `TRIP FEB 11`

##### ⑥ WHAT THE DOUBLE API WAS MISSING — worth knowing, because this paper leaned on it for a week

| Account | Rows in the GL | Rows the API returned |
|---|---:|---:|
| `Vehicle gas and fuel` | **158** | 100 |
| `Owner's distribution` | **92** | *(overflowed — never fully listed)* |

| `Car wash` | 18 | 16 |
| `Tolls` · `Auto insurance` | 21 · 5 | 21 · 5 ✅ |

🔑 **The pattern is a cap, not a filter** — the API stops at roughly 100 rows per query and pages after that
came back empty, so the shortfall lands wherever an account is busiest. ⛔ **Any figure in this paper taken
from a per-account API pull, and not re-derived from the GL, should be treated as a floor.** ✅ **Everything
in §D-6 and §D-7 has now been re-derived from the GL.**

##### ⚠️ ⑧ FIVE THINGS THE GL SHOWS THAT NOBODY HAS DECIDED — none of them blocks the entry below

   as an owner draw.** 🛠️ **Ask Julia** — it is the mirror image of the vehicle question and it moves income
   the other way.

   posted as a negative contribution)*. **Gross, the year is 101,077.40 in and 116,722.95 out.** ⚠️ **It changes
   both gross figures on Schedule M-2 line 3 and Schedule K line 16d**, and the firm's netting policy
   (§5C-v) tests the **gross** split — so this one should be grossed up before the return is built.
3. ⚠️ **1,923.80 of credit-card `STATEMENT CREDIT`s are booked as owner contributions.** A card reward or
   issuer credit is **not** money the owner put in; it is a reduction of the expense it relates to, or other
   income. **Small, and it inflates the contribution ACCOUNT — but NOT his total basis: on either of
   those readings it re-enters basis as income exactly as fast as it leaves as a contribution (§3P).**
4. ❓ **700.00 received by Zelle FROM two third parties is booked as owner contribution.** One is the
   shareholder's spouse *(consistent with a contribution)*; the other is a name that appears elsewhere in
   the year as a **payee**. **Ask whether it was a customer payment.**
5. ⚠️ **649.84 of health insurance is in distributions.** For a more-than-2% shareholder this is normally a
   company deduction reported on his W-2 — but **this company ran no payroll and issues no W-2**, so there is
   no mechanism and it stays a distribution. 🔗 **It belongs in the reasonable-compensation discussion
   (§5C-viii), not in this entry.**

#### 🎯 D-8 · WHY EACH LINE IS IN OR OUT — the evidence behind every candidate, graded, so it can be defended to Julia

🔑 **Lilian's instruction, 2026-09-02 (evening):** *"vamos a poner solo cosas que evidentemente sean gastos
relacionados con Turo… Necesito que me expliques por qué sospechas que pueden ser gastos de Turo. Explícame
bien cada uno, porque necesitamos estar bien seguros de esta journal entry… Necesito ser capaz de explicarle
bien a Yulia por qué sospecho que estos gastos pueden estar relacionados con Turo y, en lugar de ser gastos
del negocio o otro tipo de gastos."*

##### 🛑 FIRST — THE ARGUMENT IS NOT "THIS IS A TURO EXPENSE". IT IS "THIS IS NOT THE COMPANY'S VEHICLE."

**This is the single most important sentence in the section, and it makes the entry far easier to defend.**
Earlier passes framed the whole exercise as *"which costs are Turo's?"* ⛔ **That framing is weak**, because
nobody can prove from a ledger what a car was used for. **The strong framing is the one the balance sheet
gives for free:**

| The company's ENTIRE fleet, from the 2025 balance sheet | |
|---|---|
| `2014 ISU NPR TRK Salvage` | 13,000.00 |
| `2017 RAM ProMaster 3500` | 10,000.00 |
| `USED 2006 NISSAN FORKLIFT 4,000LB` | 3,700.00 |
| `2022 RAM 2500 VIN 0511` | 0.00 — disposed of in 2024, fully written off |
| **Total fixed assets** | **26,700.00** |

⛔ **Not one is a European marque. There is no Audi, no BMW, no Volkswagen** — ✅ **and none appears in the
2023 or 2024 filed returns' depreciation schedules either** *(bounded: those two returns and the 2025 books
are what was read; the company's pre-2023 history was not)*. So a part bought for an Audi, or a service
invoice from a BMW workshop, **cannot be a cost of a vehicle this company OWNS**.
🛑 **And here is the honest limit of that, because Julia can reach it in one sentence: the register proves
OWNERSHIP, not USE.** A company can legitimately pay to run a car it does not own. ✅ **Two things close the
gap:** ① **the 2025 ledger shows no vehicle lease and no long-term rental anywhere** — nothing that would
give the company the use of a passenger car; and ② 🔑 **the firm has already ruled on exactly this pattern.**
Nine `Hyundai Motor Finance` payments — **8,847.00** — were charged straight to the `Auto Expenses` parent
all year for a car that was never on the register, and **`JournalEntry-2025-ADJ` moved every cent of them to
`Owner's distribution`.** **Julia posted that entry herself.** ⚖️ **So the objection is not an objection: it
is the precedent, and this entry applies her own treatment to the vendors she had not yet reached.**
🔑 **Turo only enters the story as the REASON Julia knew the cars were personal** *(her ruling of
2026-09-01)*. **The test that keeps a line out of the company's deductions is the asset register, and it
stands on its own even if Turo had never existed.**

##### 📊 THE GRADE SCALE — three levels, and only two of them go in the entry

| Grade | What it means | Goes in the entry? |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **A — EVIDENT** | the company **cannot** have incurred it: the thing bought does not fit anything the company owns | ✅ **YES** |
| 🟡 **B — NAMED** | the descriptor **names** the thing itself *(a charge from Turo is a Turo transaction — no inference needed to say so)*, though what it was FOR still needs a decision. ⚠️ **One item, B-2, is a ONE-STEP match rather than a name** — a same-day date match — **graded B because one step is not a chain, which is what decision 14 rules out. 26.00; immaterial either way** | ✅ **YES, per Lilian's instruction** — she named these as the evident ones |
| 🔴 **C — INFERRED** | it takes a **chain** of steps to get there: descriptor → a place → out of state → not the business → personal | ⛔ **NO — noted, not moved** |

🛑 **AND HERE IS THE THING JULIA WILL ASK, SO IT IS ANSWERED BEFORE SHE DOES: these are the company RENTING a

is what Turo's support email reports. **The guest side** — paying Turo to borrow somebody else's car — is
what these 19 charges are. ⚠️ **So calling them "Turo expenses" is true in the sense that Turo is the
counterparty, and misleading if it is heard as "costs of the hosting business".**

whole Turo relationship is Artem's, not the company's** — and a car the company rented for a trip nobody has
identified as a job is not a company cost either. 🛠️ **If Julia can name a job for any of these rentals, that
one stays with the company** — it is the only line in the entry that a single sentence from her could
reverse.
⛔ **And one consequence to say out loud: moving them does NOT create a deduction on Artem's Schedule C.**
Renting a car from Turo is not a cost of hosting cars on Turo. **Unless he can show a business purpose, the
1,447.68 is simply non-deductible personal travel.**

##### 🧭 LILIAN'S CEILING TEST — the right instinct, and the honest answer about where it applies

**Her point, verbatim:** *"los gastos de Turo no pueden exceder los gastos que dio el Customer Support de la
plataforma, porque se supone que en esa plataforma está el máximo de los gastos… si es un pago de otra cosa
fuera de la plataforma, pero que está relacionado con Turo, lo podremos analizar."*
*(⚠️ The dictation wrote "DigTech" for "Turo" in two places; read as Turo throughout — the sentence about the
platform's customer support makes it unambiguous.)*

✅ **The instinct is right, and it is a real control.** ⚠️ **But it caps a different set of numbers than the
ones in this entry, and the difference matters:**

| What Turo's support email reports | Amount | Is it capped by the report? |
|---|---:|---|
| **Gross host earnings** | 10,800.78 | ✅ this IS the report — it is Artem's Schedule C line 1 |
| **Turo's own fee** | (2,601.19) | ✅ **the ceiling test works perfectly here** — the platform's commission is the only cost Turo itself charged, and nothing else billed *through the platform* can exceed what the platform says it billed |
| **Reimbursements** | 628.15 | ✅ capped by the report, and neither income nor deduction |
| 🔴 **The company's 1,447.68 of `TURO INC.* TRIP` charges** | 1,447.68 | ⛔ **NOT capped — and this is the point.** These are the company as a **guest**. They never appear in a host earnings report, so the report is silent on them |
| 🔴 **The European-car costs** *(gross of the misposted refund; 9,148.81 net of it)* | 10,236.75 | ⛔ **NOT capped either** — they were paid to dealers and parts sellers **off the platform**. Turo never saw them |

🔑 **So the ceiling test protects the Schedule C and does not reach this return.** ⚠️ **What it DOES give
this return is a plausibility reading, and it is worth knowing before Julia asks:** the whole-year European
car cost, net of refunds, is **9,148.81 against gross host earnings of 10,800.78 — about 85%.** ⛔ **That is
not proof of anything** *(cars genuinely being repaired can cost that)*, **but it is the size of the thing,
and it is why the costs were worth questioning in the first place.**

entire fleet is a 2014 Isuzu NPR box truck, a 2017 RAM ProMaster van and a

09/01/2026 that the passenger cars and the whole Turo relationship are
personal to the family, not the company's. Personal costs an S corporation
pays for its shareholder are distributions, not deductible expenses
(IRC 262; 1368).

TO WHOM. Ishkhan Markarian, the sole shareholder for the whole of 2025 - he
holds 100% for all 365 days, and the transfer to Artem Markarian is effective
at the close of 12/31/2025. Payments that benefited Artem are the
shareholder's own money spent for his son and are NOT re-characterised.

THE AMOUNTS.
 1,692.40  Vehicles repair - the European-marque charges left in the account:
           10,236.75 for the year, less the 7,456.41 moved by JE 2025-ADJ on
           12/31/2025, less the 1,087.94 Audi Jacksonville refund returned to
           this account by the correcting entry of the same date.
   175.00  Software - five monthly charges to Partslink24, the OEM parts
           catalogue for European marques, April to August 2025.
 1,447.68  Taxi or shared rides - the company's 19 Turo charges, net of
           reversals and rebills, February to August 2025.
    26.00  Auto insurance - MBA Rental Insurance, 03/12/2025, bought the same
           day as two Turo rentals.

WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT HERE.
 - The 8,847.00 of vehicle finance, 7,456.41 of vehicle repairs and 1,804.12
   already moved to this account by JE 2025-ADJ on 12/31/2025. Repeating them
   would double-count the distribution.
 - 1,192.24 of fuel, parking and insurance premiums that look personal but
   rest on inference rather than evidence (453.86 fuel at out-of-state
   brands, 107.03 of Miami-area parking, 631.35 of Infinity premiums). These
   stay as company expenses until the insurance declaration pages are seen.
 - Everything left in account 43 after this entry - 4,059.65 - is the
   forklift dealer, generic auto parts, three individuals paid by Zelle and a
   vehicle wrap, none of which is a European-marque cost.

EFFECT. No cash moves. Deductible expenses fall and ordinary income rises by
3,341.08; distributions rise by the same amount. Under IRC 1367 the two
cancel, so the shareholder's stock basis is unchanged and no capital gain
arises. Cost of goods sold and Form 1125-A are untouched.

Prepared by JK Accounting Group, 2026-09-02, for the 2025 Form 1120-S
(cash basis, following the 2024 return).

#### 🔄 D-10 · WHY TURO MONEY GOES OUT AND COMES BACK — the same amount, twice, and what it actually means

🔑 **Lilian's question, 2026-09-02 (evening), with the QuickBooks banking screen attached:** *"hay
transacciones entrantes y salientes… Algunas de ellas son por el mismo monto, es decir, el mismo dinero que
se pagó luego volvió a entrar… necesito entender por qué el dinero entra y sale por el mismo monto."*

##### 🔑 THE ANSWER IN ONE MOVE: STOP READING THE ROWS, READ THE **TRIPS**

**Every Turo row but two carries the trip it belongs to inside its own descriptor** — `TRIP FEB 8`,
`TRIP MAR 12`, `TRIP AUG 20`. ⚠️ *(The exception is the June rebill pair, truncated at `TRIP MA` — below.)* ⛔ **Read as 19 rows it looks like chaos. Grouped by trip it is five rentals that cost money — one of them with
a partial refund — plus two bookings reversed in full the next day and one same-day billing retry that
collected nothing.**

| The trip | Rows | What happened | **Net cost** |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| **`TRIP FEB 8`** | 2 | charged **02-08** 269.71 → **refunded in full 02-09**, the next day | 🔵 **0.00 — the rental was cancelled** |
| **`TRIP FEB 10`** | 2 | two charges the same day, 7.59 + 121.50 | **129.09** |
| **`TRIP FEB 11`** | 1 | one charge | **234.42** |
| **`TRIP MAR 2`** | 5 | charged **03-01** 349.20, **03-04** 80.84, **03-15** 4.57 — then a **247.45 credited 06-03 and re-charged 06-04**, which nets to nothing | **434.61** |
| **`TRIP MAR 12`** | 3 | 188.61 + 182.73 on the day, plus 68.42 on **04-08** | **439.76** |
| **`TRIP MAR 14`** | 2 | charged **03-13** 959.80 → **750.00 refunded 03-18**: a **partial** refund | **209.80** |
| **the 06-03 `REBILL` / `CREDIT` pair** | 2 | a 247.45 rebilled and credited the **same day** | 🔵 **0.00 — a billing retry** |
| **`TRIP AUG 20`** | 2 | charged **08-20** 456.51 → **refunded in full 08-21**, the next day | 🔵 **0.00 — the rental was cancelled** |
| | **19** | **five trips actually cost money; three sets cancel out** | **1,447.68** |

✅ **1,447.68 is the whole of it, and now it is five rentals rather than nineteen mystery rows.**

##### 🧭 THE THREE THINGS THAT MAKE MONEY COME BACK, AND HOW TO TELL THEM APART

| Pattern | What it looks like in the feed | Here |
|---|---|---|
| **① A CANCELLED RENTAL** | a charge, then a credit for **exactly the same amount**, **within a day or two**, **carrying the same trip label** | `TRIP FEB 8` *(269.71, 02-08 → 02-09)* and `TRIP AUG 20` *(456.51, 08-20 → 08-21)*. ⚠️ **A cancellation is the natural reading of a card feed — but both charges post ON the trip date itself, so "called off before it ran" is NOT established: a cancellation after the start, a host cancellation and a disputed charge look identical here. The net is 0.00 on every reading, which is what makes the hedge free** |
| **② A BILLING RETRY** | a charge and a credit **on the same day**, often with the word **REBILL** or **CREDIT** written into the descriptor by the processor itself | the 06-03 cluster — **four rows of 247.45, two out and two in, all inside two days.** ⚠️ **Two of them are matched to `TRIP MAR 2` by the AMOUNT, not the label — their descriptors are truncated at `TRIP MA`, which could equally be MAR 2, MAR 12 or MAR 14.** *(It changes nothing: all four net to zero whichever trip they belong to.)* ⚠️ **The sequence even starts with a credit**, which cannot precede its own charge; that is the bank posting same-day items in whatever order it likes. **What matters is that it nets to zero: Turo tried to collect 247.45 for the March 2 trip three months later and, after two attempts, collected nothing** |
| **③ A PARTIAL REFUND** | a charge, then a credit for **less** than the charge | `TRIP MAR 14` — **959.80 charged, 750.00 back, 209.80 kept.** **This one is a real cost and it stays** |

🛑 **THE PRACTICAL RULE, AND IT IS THE POINT OF THE WHOLE SECTION: never move a Turo row on its own — move
the trip.** ⛔ **The 19 rows are 14 charges totalling 3,418.80 and 5 credits totalling 1,971.12.** **Anyone
who reclassifies the charges and misses the credits moves 3,418.80 instead of 1,447.68 — overstating the
distribution by 1,971.12 — **more than the 1,447.68 line it is meant to move**, and more than half the whole
entry.** ✅ **The 1,447.68 in the §D-8 entry is the NET, so it is already right — but the check when it is
posted is that `Taxi or shared rides` lands on 1,058.57, not that any particular number of rows
disappeared.** *(Eight of the nineteen wash to zero inside their own trip: the two `FEB 8` rows, the two
`AUG 20` rows, the `REBILL`/`CREDIT` pair, and the 06-03/06-04 pair inside `TRIP MAR 2`.)*

##### ✅ THE ENTRY ALREADY DOES WHAT LILIAN ASKED — no second journal entry is needed

**Her instruction:** *"tenemos que crear una Journal Entry para mover eso de sitio, porque están ahora en
taxis Unshared Rides, pero hay que moverlo."* ✅ **That is line 4 of the §D-8 entry** — `Cr Taxi or shared
rides 1,447.68` — **so it is already written and no separate entry is required.** ⚠️ **What §D-8 does NOT
change is the account's remaining balance:** after the entry `Taxi or shared rides` still holds **1,058.57**
of Uber, Metro-North, OMNY and a trailer rental. ⛔ **Those are not Turo and do not move** *(§D-2b ①)*.

**The banking screen lists 24 Turo transactions; the 2025 General Ledger holds 19.** ✅ **The other five are
2024:** `TRIP JUL 3` **16.74** *(07-27-2024)* · **30.90** *(07-20-2024)* · **197.51** *(07-11-2024)* ·
**25.07** *(05-13-2024)* · **9.63** *(05-05-2024)* — **279.85 in total, all posted to `Taxi or shared rides`
in 2024.** 🔑 **Two things follow, and both are worth writing down rather than acting on:**
1. ⚠️ **The 2024 BOOKS therefore carried Turo guest rentals in a deductible account.** *(Whether the filed
   2024 return actually deducted them is an inference — this client's year-end entries do move blocks out,
   as §D-0b shows.)* **2024 is filed and
   closed** — 🛑 **and per Lilian's standing scope rule this is recorded once and NOT pursued**
   *(her wording, 2026-09-02, said of the 2023 return: **"Tratemos de no buscar errores en esta declaración, a
   menos que nos influya para la de 2025, porque ya está cerrada."** **Applying it to 2024 as the same
   principle is this paper's step, not a quotation.**)*. **It does not
   affect 2025.**
2. 🔑 **It weakens the reading that these rentals are one personal Florida trip, and strengthens a plainer
   one: this company rents cars from Turo from time to time, and has since at least May 2024.** ⚖️ **It does
   not change what happens on this return** — Julia's ruling sends the Turo relationship to Artem either way
   — **but it is the honest shape of the fact, and it is exactly the sort of thing she may want to weigh.**
⚠️ *One small bookkeeping detail visible in the same screen: the 2024 rows carry **no vendor** while the 2025
rows are all assigned to `Turo Inc.` Immaterial to the return; it is why a vendor-based search finds the 2025
rows and not the 2024 ones.*

gross figures)*, **but the test should be applied to the right numbers.**

basis**.
🛑 **REWRITTEN 2026-09-02 (night), after review — the three things this paragraph used to say were all
wrong, and each is worth naming because each is an easy mistake:**
- ⛔ ~~"1,923.80 of card credits is basis he did not create"~~ — **no.** On this note's own two readings a
  card credit is either **a reduction of the expense that earned it** or **other income**, and **both
  re-enter basis at step 2 exactly as fast as they leave step 1** *(§3P)*. 🔑 **What is wrong is the
  ACCOUNT, not the amount** — his total stock basis is the same either way. **The one reading that would
  cut it is a LIABILITY**, which §4 decision 11 closed and Schedule L line 19 reflects.
- ⛔ ~~"there is none on any reading"~~ — **not any more.** ✅ **There is no capital gain at depreciation
  = 0**, **but a MACRS or bonus deduction above about 21,358.55 creates one — up to 5,341.45** *(§3H)*.
  🛠️ **§3P is the authority; do not answer that question from this note.**
- ⛔ ~~"it carries into 2026 on his Form 7203"~~ — **it does not.** 🔑 **His K-1 is FINAL** *(§3N)*, so
  nothing in **his** basis carries into 2026. ⚠️ **And do not replace that with "Artem's basis is created
  at the transfer" either** — **§B-2 says it depends on HOW the shares moved**, which is still open: **what
  he paid** on a sale or a redemption, **his father's basis carried over under §1015** on a gift, and the
  **dual-basis rule of Reg. §1.1015-4** on a bargain sale — *which §B-2 calls the common case between
  family members.*

⚖️ **Left as posted, per Lilian** *(§4 decisions 15 and 17)*.

##### 🩺 NOTE 4 · The health-insurance premiums — the detail Lilian asked for, and why the above-the-line deduction is blocked

🔑 **Her request:** *"me das detalles de esa cuenta porque probablemente lo aplique como un above-the-line
deduction. Buscá lo que creas que puede ser pago de seguro médico."*

**Every health-insurance row in the whole General Ledger — searched across all 51 accounts, not just the
draw account:**

| Date | Amount | Descriptor | Posted to |
|---|---:|---|---|
| 2025-01-30 | 162.46 | `OSCAR INSURANCE COMPANY` | `Owner's distribution` |
| 2025-02-28 | 162.46 | `OSCAR INSURANCE COMPANY` | `Owner's distribution` |
| 2025-03-31 | 162.46 | `OSCAR INSURANCE COMPANY` | `Owner's distribution` |
| 2025-04-30 | 162.46 | `OSCAR INSURANCE COMPANY` | `Owner's distribution` |
| 2025-05-30 | (1,947.46) | `oscarhealth` — a **credit** | `Owner's distribution` |
| 2025-05-31 | 1,947.46 | `oscarhealth` — re-charged | `Owner's distribution` |
| | **649.84 net for the year** | | |

**Three things to read off that table:**
1. ✅ **The insurer is Oscar Insurance Company — a health-insurance marketplace carrier.** So the policy is
   almost certainly an **ACA marketplace plan**, which means a **Form 1095-A** should exist for the household,
   and Artem's or Ishkhan's 1040 may carry a **Form 8962** premium-tax-credit reconciliation. 🔗 **That is a
   1040 matter and it belongs in the handoff, not on this return.**
2. 🔴 **The premiums stop after April.** Four monthly charges at 162.46, then nothing for the rest of the
   year on the company's cards. **Either the coverage ended, or it moved to a payment method these books do
   not see.** ⚠️ **A gap in coverage months is exactly what Form 8962 reconciles against.**
3. ⚠️ **The 1,947.46 pair washes to zero** — credited 05-30, charged 05-31. **Net effect nil**, but it is a
   large figure and it looks like a reversal-and-repost of an annual or catch-up payment. **Worth one look at
   the May statement if the 1095-A does not tie.**

🛑 **NOW THE HARD PART, AND IT IS THE ANSWER TO HER "probably an above-the-line deduction":**
**For a MORE-THAN-2% S-CORPORATION SHAREHOLDER the §162(l) self-employed health-insurance deduction is
NOT automatic — it is gated on the W-2.** The rule *(IRS Notice 2008-1)* is that the premiums must be **paid
or reimbursed by the S corporation AND included in the shareholder's Form W-2 wages**; the shareholder then
takes the deduction on the front of the 1040. ⛔ **This company ran no payroll in 2025 and issues no W-2**
(§6 blocker A) — **so there is no W-2 in which to include them, and the above-the-line deduction has no
mechanism.** ✅ **Which is why leaving the premiums in `Owner's distribution` is the correct treatment on
these facts, not a compromise.**
🔗 **It is not dead, though — it is CHAINED to the reasonable-compensation decision (§5C-viii).** **If the
firm decides to run late payroll for 2025, the same 649.84 can be included in the W-2 and becomes deductible
on the 1040 at the same time.** 🛠️ **So the two decisions should be made together, not separately.**
✅ **VERIFIED against irs.gov, 2026-09-02.** **Notice 2008-1**: *"the S corporation must report the accident
and health insurance premiums paid or reimbursed as wages on the 2-percent shareholder-employee's Form W-2 in
that same year."*
🛑 **And two further gates the deduction must pass even once a W-2 exists — worth knowing before anyone plans
around it:** **§162(l)(2)(A)** caps it at the **earned income from that trade or business** *(with no wages,
earned income from this S corporation is nil — a second reason it fails today)*, and **§162(l)(2)(B)** denies
it **for any month the taxpayer is eligible for a subsidised plan of his own or his spouse's employer.**

#### D-3 · How it is presented IF it belongs on this return

🛑 **Report the GROSS and expense the fee. Never the net.** Netting understates revenue *and* expenses
by the same 2,601.19 and misstates gross receipts — the figure a 1099-K would have matched to, had one

#### 🔴 E-3 · A NEGATIVE AAA AGAINST 115,722.95 OF DISTRIBUTIONS — ~~this is the real exposure~~ 🛑 *(it is not — corrected below: basis decides, and there is basis left)*

**Opening AAA is (89,293). 2025 distributions are 115,722.95.**

🛑 **CORRECTED — read this before using the figures.** An earlier version of this section said the AAA
*"cannot absorb"* the distributions and that *"the excess"* reduces basis. **That is the classic error the
SOP names** ([`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §10.0b: *"The AAA decides
PRESENTATION. Basis decides TAX"*). **With no accumulated E&P — which is settled below — AAA has NOTHING
to do with whether the distribution is taxable:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **§1368(b)(1)** | the **WHOLE** 115,722.95 reduces each shareholder's **stock basis**, pro rata to what they received — **not the excess over AAA** |
| **§1368(b)(2)** | **anything beyond that shareholder's basis is CAPITAL GAIN** |
| **§1368(c)** | the AAA-based ordering — **does not apply here**, because it requires accumulated E&P and there is none |

🔑 **So the real question is "does either owner have basis left?", and it belongs to whoever the
distributions actually went to.** What decides it is **each shareholder's Form 7203 basis**, on their own
1040 — which the per-shareholder split (blocker B) has to be built before anyone can compute. 🛑 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-02: one shareholder, no split — the question is the sole shareholder's basis — **ISHKHAN's**, on Julia's date (§B-0) — and under reading ① of §B-1 there is basis left and no gain.**

⚠️ **What the negative AAA does do is PRESENTATION, and it looks misleading.** AAA cannot be driven
negative by distributions — the excess never enters Schedule M-2 (SOP §10A) — so **2025's M-2 line 7 will
report at or near zero while 16d and the K-1 *(one, since 2026-09-02)* carry the full gross.** 🛑 **That is not the netting
presentation this paper ruled out; do not let it be read as one.**

⚠️ **And the 2024 return gives some of the company-side inputs for Ishkhan's basis, though not the
answer:** 100% of a (68,058) ordinary loss and a (18,847) §1231 loss, against contributions that are
**between 46,446 and 60,656 and not pinned down** (§E-2 ②). ⛔ **His actual opening basis is on HIS 2024
Form 7203, filed with his own 1040, not here.**

| # | What ATX asks for | Have it? | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|---|

| 4 | 🟡 **BUSINESS-USE PERCENTAGE** | ⛔ **no** | ⚠️ **CHECK THE EXCEPTION BEFORE ASKING THE CLIENT FOR MILEAGE LOGS.** A vehicle that is a **qualified nonpersonal-use vehicle** — §280F(d)(4)(C) / Reg. §1.274-5T(k): a delivery truck with seating only for the driver, and similar — **is NOT listed property and needs no mileage substantiation at all.** 🔑 **A 2014 Isuzu NPR box truck and a RAM ProMaster 3500 cargo van are strong candidates**, and a **forklift is not a passenger automobile in any case.** 🛠️ **Check seating, permanent markings and GVWR first.** ⛔ **Only if they fail that test do you need the business / commuting / personal split** — and then note that no log has been seen and the 2024 return already failed this test (§E-6) |
| 5 | **Recovery period / class** | 🟡 derivable | a **heavy truck** is normally **5-year**. ⚠️ **The forklift is NOT automatically 7-year:** for a **construction trade** — which is what this client is — equipment falls in Rev. Proc. 87-56 **asset class 15.0**, giving **5-year** GDS. **"7-year" is the generic-business answer, not this client's.** ⚠️ **Confirm against the asset, do not assume either** |
| 6 | 🔴 **THE ELECTION — §179, bonus, or plain MACRS** | ⛔ **a DECISION** | **yours or Julia's.** It moves this year's deduction, the K-1, and every future year |

⚠️ **THE TRAP IN COLUMN 2, and it is the one that costs money.** *"Cost"* on a return means **everything
paid to get the asset in service** — purchase price **plus** sales tax, title, registration, freight and
any work done to make it usable. **The book account may hold only what was Zelle'd to the seller.** For
the forklift the books show 3,700.00 as two payments to one vendor; **if anything else was paid for that
machine it belongs in the basis and is not in that account.** 🛠️ **Ask the client for the invoices.**

⚠️ **And one more on the ProMaster: 7,000.00 of its 10,000.00 came in through a journal entry against a
loan, not through a payment.** **That is normal for a financed vehicle** — the basis is the **full
price**, whether paid in cash or borrowed — **but confirm the 10,000.00 is the whole price and not just
the part that ran through the books.** 🔑 **A financed asset's cost is the price, not the deposit.**

the business?** The paper's whole vehicle analysis (§D-4) is about personal use running through this
company. **These three are the working fleet on their face** — a box truck, a cargo van and a forklift,

##### 🔴 THE ONE THING THAT CHANGES 2025 — the opening AAA is wrong on the 2024 return

**2023's Schedule M-2, and it is internally exact:**

| Line | | |
|---|---|---:|
| 1 | Balance at beginning of 2023 | **(3,129)** |
| 3 | Other additions | 300 |
| 4 | Loss from page 1 line 22 | **(11,034)** |
| 5 | Other reductions | (347) |
| 6 / 8 | **Balance at END of 2023** | **(14,210)** |

✅ **−3,129 + 300 − 11,034 − 347 = −14,210 exactly**, and 2023's Schedule L retained earnings ends at
**(14,210)** too — the two agree.

🛑 **AND THE 2024 RETURN OPENED ITS SCHEDULE M-2 AT ZERO.** It should have opened at **(14,210)**.
**So the 2024 ending AAA of (89,293) is the year's movement only — it is short by 14,210.**

| | |
|---|---:|
| 2024 M-2 as filed, ending balance | (89,293) |
| **should be** (14,210) + (89,293) | **(103,503)** |
| **understated by** | **14,210** |

🔑 **This is exactly the kind of thing Lilian said to bring up: it changes a figure on the 2025 return.**
**Schedule M-2 line 1 for 2025 is one of two numbers, and choosing is a decision, not a computation:**

| Option | What it means |
|---|---|
| **Open at (103,503)** — the arithmetically correct figure | the AAA is right going forward; it does **not** agree with the prior filed return, and the 14,210 is an unexplained jump a reviewer would ask about |
| **Open at (89,293)** — the prior return's ending balance | continuity with what was filed; the AAA stays 14,210 understated for good |

⚠️ **How much does it actually matter? Less than it looks, and say so honestly.** The AAA is deeply
negative either way, so **M-2 line 7 reports zero either way**, the distributions are **basis**-driven
either way (blocker B), and **taxable income does not move at all.** 🔑 **It changes one line on one
schedule.** ⛔ **But it should be a decision someone made, not a number nobody noticed.**

⚖️ **DECIDED 2026-09-02 — LILIAN: OPEN AT (89,293).** *"Vamos a mantener esa cifra subestimada porque no
quiero que haya incongruencias entre un año y el otro."* ✅ **That is also the SOP default** —
[`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §10.0b *(the line-1 row: "last year's FILED return, its own line 8. Copied, never recalculated")* and §10.1 rule 1 *("Beginning balance = last year's ending balance, from the filed return. Copy it")* — so the return follows the house rule as written *(⚠️ the SOP says nothing about a prior line 8
known to be wrong; this paper does)* and the 14,210 stays a known, recorded understatement.
(§4 decision 2.)

##### 🔍 E-8e · Lilian's question — *"¿Dónde puedes ver eso en los libros? ¿Por qué sabes que debería ser 103,503?"*

**Short answer: you cannot see it in the books, because the AAA is not a book figure.** It exists only
on Schedule M-2 of the return; QuickBooks has no AAA account. **The evidence is the two filed returns
side by side:**

| Where | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **2023 return, Schedule M-2 line 8** | AAA at the end of 2023: **(14,210)** — and the schedule is internally exact (above) |
| **2024 return, Schedule M-2 line 1** | AAA at the start of 2024: **0** — 🛑 **it should be the previous line: line 1 of a year is line 8 of the year before, by construction** |
| **2024 return, Schedule L, retained earnings, beginning of year** | **(14,210)** — 🔑 **the same return carried the number on one schedule and dropped it on the other** |
| **The books — the only corroboration they can give** | retained earnings at 2024-01-01 = **(13,908.58)** *(§5 tie-out: (13,908.58) + (89,292.05) = (103,200.63))* — **about the same 14 thousand, 301.42 apart** *(the 301.42 is the book-vs-return difference already recorded in §5)*. **The books say a deficit of that size existed before 2024; they cannot say what the AAA was** |

So **(89,293) is only 2024's own movement** *(2024's loss and adjustments)*, and **(103,503) = (14,210)
+ (89,293)** is what line 1 would have produced had it been copied. ✅ **Why keeping (89,293) costs
nothing in tax:** the AAA is deeply negative either way, M-2 line 7 reports zero either way, and the
distributions are taxed on **basis**, not AAA (E-3). **One line, one schedule, no tax on this return — while the AAA is negative and E&P is nil.**
⚠️ **Scope, written down so it is not forgotten:** the 14,210 carries forward as a known **overstatement of
the AAA balance** *(the deficit is understated — "subestimada", Lilian's word — which is the same fact
seen from the other side)*. It would only ever bite if the AAA turned positive: in a **post-termination
transition period**, where §1371(e) makes distributions tax-free only up to the AAA, or if **accumulated
E&P** ever arose (§1368(c)). Neither is in sight; both are the reason the understatement is recorded
rather than dropped.

##### 📘 E-8f · Lilian's questions on Schedule M-2 — the theory in plain words, and the "not required" hypothesis tested

**Her questions, 2026-09-02:** *"¿Hay alguna posibilidad de que en la declaración del 2024 el AAA esté en
cero porque no había obligación de someter el Schedule M-2 en ese año?… Explícame por qué dices que hay una
incongruencia… no entiendo las fórmulas, la teoría detrás de este Schedule."*

**1 · What the AAA is, without formulas.** An S corporation pays no tax itself; its profit is taxed to the
shareholders through the K-1 every year, whether or not they take the money out. The **AAA** is the
corporation's **running tally of profit already taxed to the shareholders and not yet paid out to them**.
It is not cash and it is not a book account — QuickBooks has no such account — it is a memo balance kept
**on the return**, on Schedule M-2 column (a). Its only job is to answer, when money is paid out: *"has this
already been taxed?"* If yes, the distribution is tax-free up to that balance.

**2 · How the schedule moves, year by year.** Start with last year's ending balance. Add this year's taxable
profit. Subtract this year's loss, the separately stated deductions *(§179, charitable)* and the expenses
the law never lets anyone deduct. Subtract the distributions — **but distributions can never push it below
zero**; the part they cannot absorb simply is not entered. *(One refinement from the instructions, for
completeness: in a year where the decreases exceed the increases, that "net negative adjustment" is taken
last, after the distributions — SOP §10.0b simplifies it, and it makes no difference to this return, where
2025 is a profit year and the 2024 reconstruction carries no distributions.)* What is left is the ending balance, **and that ending balance is next year's starting
balance.** *(In the form's numbering: line 1 → lines 2–5 → line 6 → line 7 → line 8, and line 8 becomes
the following year's line 1.)* The IRS instructions describe it as one account: it is **zero only "on the
first day of the corporation's first tax year as an S corporation"**, and every year *"at the end of the
tax year, adjust the AAA for the items… in the order listed"* *(2025 Instructions for Form 1120-S, Schedule
M-2, column (a))*. **Losses CAN drive it negative** — which is why this company's is negative: 2023 lost
money, 2024 lost more.

**3 · When it matters, and when it does not.** It matters when the corporation has **accumulated earnings
and profits** from years as a C corporation — then the AAA is what separates a tax-free distribution from
a taxable dividend — and in a **post-termination transition period**. **This company has no E&P** (§E-2 ④,
corroborated), so for it the AAA decides **nothing about tax**: whether a 2025 distribution is taxable is
decided by **the shareholder's basis — ISHKHAN's, on Julia's date (§B-0)** — and the AAA is a schedule that has to be filled in correctly and
consistently, nothing more. That is why keeping (89,293) costs nothing (§E-8e).

**4 · Why it is an inconsistency, and not an opinion.** One account cannot have two balances at the same
instant. The 2023 return says the balance at **the end of 2023** was **(14,210)**. The 2024 return says the
balance at **the beginning of 2024** was **0**. Those are the same moment — midnight, 31 December 2023 —
and the two returns disagree by 14,210. **The same 2024 return then carries (14,210) as the opening retained
earnings on its Schedule L**, so the preparer's file knew the deficit existed; it simply did not land on M-2
line 1. The consequence is mechanical: 2024's ending (89,293) is 2024's own movement alone, and the true
ending balance is (103,503).

**5 · Her hypothesis — "the 2024 zero could be because the schedule was not required that year" — tested
against the instructions, and it does not hold, for four separate reasons:**

| | |
|---|---|
| ① **The small-company exemption does not cover M-2.** | Schedule B question 11 *(the form's own words: receipts and year-end assets both under 250,000)* exempts **Schedule L and Schedule M-1 only** — the 2025 instructions say, for each of the two, that it *"isn't required to be completed if the corporation answered 'Yes' to question 11 on Schedule B."* **No such sentence exists for Schedule M-2.** *(Read off `i1120s.pdf` from irs.gov on 2026-09-02; the 2024 edition carried the same rule — stated from memory, the exemption has been stable for years.)* |
| ② **What the instructions DO allow is narrower, and it is not "start at zero".** | For a corporation **without** E&P they say it *"doesn't need to maintain the AAA in order to determine the tax effect of distributions"* — and two sentences later that *"it is recommended that the AAA be maintained by all S corporations."* Not maintaining it would mean leaving the column alone; **it never means resetting a balance that exists.** The only zero the instructions prescribe is the first day of the **first** S year. |
| ③ **2024 was not the first S year, and its M-2 was not left blank.** | The 2023 return is itself a Form 1120-S with a completed Schedule M-2 ending at (14,210) — so the S election predates 2024. And the 2024 M-2 **was filled in**: it carries the year's loss and other reductions and ends at (89,293). A schedule that was completed, with a wrong line 1, is not a schedule that was omitted. |
| ④ **Even on the return that filed it, the zero disagrees with itself.** | 2024's Schedule L opens retained earnings at (14,210) — the deficit was in the file; it did not reach M-2 line 1. |

🔑 **The most likely mechanical cause — a hypothesis, not established:** the 2024 return was prepared in a
file that did not carry the 2023 ending balance forward *(a fresh file, a software change, or a roll-over
step that was not run)*, so ATX opened M-2 at its default of zero and nobody typed (14,210) over it.
🛠️ **It is checkable in ATX in one look:** does the 2024 file's Schedule M-2 show a prior-year carry-over
field, and is it empty?

⚖️ **The decision stands** — 2025 opens at (89,293) on Lilian's instruction (§4 decision 2), and this
section exists so the reason the figure is "wrong but kept" is understood, not just recorded.

## 9 · Provenance

> 🔑 **2026-09-05 — a SHAREHOLDER's own document entered this paper, and it is worth naming here.**
> Lilian sent **Ishkhan Markarian's filed 2024 Form 7203** *(regular and AMT)* and asked what it does to
> his basis. **It was read under the 2026-09-04 permission** — *"tienes derecho a acceder a los
> documentos siempre que yo te lo pida"* — **by her direct request, in her own session, with her
> present.** ⛔ **Not from a subagent, not from a scheduled run, and not into the working tree**: the

**2026-09-02, seventh pass — the review round.** The independent review found four blocking items and they
are fixed here, three of which change what goes on a return:
- 🛑 **The BASIS ORDERING was wrong.** This paper had said 2025's income frees the suspended 2024 loss
  *before* the distributions. **Form 7203's own instructions put distributions SECOND and losses FOURTH**
  (Reg. §1.1367-1(f)), so the loss reaches only the basis that survives 115,722.95 of distributions. Corrected
  in §B-0, §B-2 and the father's client file; the Reg. §1.1367-1(g) election is named for what it does and
  does not do.
- 🆕 **A fourth reading in §B-1: the shareholder's own money LENT** — an open-account advance rather than a
  contribution. It is the shape the section itself describes, and its consequences differ in kind: debt basis
  on Form 7203 Part II, a Schedule L line 19 liability, and **income on repayment of basis-reduced debt**
  where reading ① reports a tax-free distribution.
- 🛑 **§4's "nothing else on this return waits on it" was wrong** — a **redemption** does reach this return.
- **§D-6c's effects table is labelled reading-① only**, with the two variants spelled out; **decision 10's
  closure of "N" propagated** through STEP 0, the floor paragraph and the entry block; the **§302 waiver
  conditions** and the client's own facts against them written in; a **bargain sale** branch added; the two
  "stated from memory" claims **verified against the 2025 Instructions for Form 709 on irs.gov** and two
  corrections made *(the gift-tax test is DOMICILE, not income-tax residency; a taxable gift by such a person
  is Form 709-NA)*; the **§199A carryforward and the suspended loss flagged as mutually constraining**;
  **K-1 Item H** named as the entry point that decides the allocated percentage; and the "the transcript won"
  framing narrowed to the exit date.

  Lilian asked about: it is blocked, and the reason is the W-2** — a more-than-2% shareholder's premiums are
  deductible under §162(l) only if the S corporation includes them in his W-2 wages, and this company ran no
  payroll. **It is chained to the reasonable-compensation decision, so the two should be made together.**

  1,447.68, two cancelled bookings refunded the next day, and one same-day billing retry** — plus one partial
  refund that leaves a real 209.80. **The practical rule is written down: move the trip, never the row** —
  14 charges total 3,418.80 against 5 credits of 1,971.12, so anyone moving only the charges overstates the
  distribution by more than the very line it is meant to move. ✅ **And no second journal entry is needed: §D-8 line 4 already
  moves the net.**
